   564	    /// @dev Shared BLS decode + threshold check + verify helper.
   565	    function _verifyBLS(bytes calldata proof, bytes32 messageHash) internal {
   566	        (uint256 signerMask, bytes memory sigG2Bytes) = abi.decode(proof, (uint256, bytes));
   567	        address agg = blsAggregator;
   568	        uint256 threshold = IBLSAggregator(agg).defaultThreshold();
   569	        uint256 _m = signerMask; uint256 _bits; while (_m != 0) { _m &= (_m - 1); _bits++; }
   570	        if (_bits < threshold) revert InsufficientConsensus();
   571	        if (!IBLSAggregator(agg).verify(messageHash, signerMask, threshold, sigG2Bytes)) revert BLSFailed();
   572	    }
   573	
   574	    function batchUpdateGlobalReputation(
   575	        uint256 proposalId,
   576	        address[] calldata users,
   577	        uint256[] calldata newScores,
   578	        uint256 epoch,
   579	        bytes calldata proof
   580	    ) external nonReentrant {
   581	        if (!isReputationSource[msg.sender]) revert UnauthorizedSource();
   582	        if (users.length != newScores.length) revert LenMismatch();
   583	        if (users.length > 200) revert BatchTooLarge();
   584	        if (proof.length == 0) revert BLSProofRequired();
   585	        if (blsAggregator == address(0)) revert BLSNotConfigured();
   586	        if (proposalId == 0) revert InvalidProposalId();
   587	        // L-C: epoch=0 would skip every user (epoch <= lastReputationEpoch, default 0)
   588	        // yet still burn the proposalId below — a permanently wasted, un-retryable proposal.
   589	        if (epoch == 0) revert InvalidParam();
   590	        // CC-48 round-9 MEDIUM-HIGH-B3: a freshly upgraded proxy reads zero in the
   591	        // population slots while real, already-promoted users exist on-chain. Deriving
   592	        // exposure from that empty population would under-count the live stock by exactly
   593	        // the pre-upgrade issuance, so the reputation path stays SHUT until governance has
   594	        // seeded the population and declared it complete. Fresh deployments are seeded in
   595	        // `initialize`, where the population provably IS empty.
   596	        if (creditPopulationSeededAt == 0) revert CreditPopulationNotSeeded();
   597	        if (executedProposals[proposalId]) revert ProposalAlreadyExecuted();
   598	
   599	        executedProposals[proposalId] = true;
   600	        _verifyBLS(proof, _reputationMessageHash(proposalId, users, newScores, epoch));
   601	
   602	        uint256 aggregateUplift;
   603	        uint256 aggregateRelease;
   604	        uint256 backfill;
   605	        uint256 marker = creditPopulationEpoch + 1;
   606	        uint256 floorLimit = creditTierConfig[1];
   607	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < users.length; ) {
   608	            address user = users[i];
   609	            if (epoch <= lastReputationEpoch[user]) {
   610	                unchecked { ++i; }
   611	                continue;
   612	            }
   613	            uint256 _old = globalReputation[user]; uint256 _new = newScores[i];
   614	            uint256 clamped = (_new > _old)
   615	                ? ((_new - _old > 100) ? _old + 100 : _new)
   616	                : ((_old > _new && _old - _new > 100) ? _old - 100 : _new);
   617	            uint256 oldLimit = creditTierConfig[_levelForReputation(_old)];
   618	            uint256 newLimit = creditTierConfig[_levelForReputation(clamped)];
   619	            if (newLimit > oldLimit) aggregateUplift += newLimit - oldLimit;
   620	            else if (oldLimit > newLimit) aggregateRelease += oldLimit - newLimit;
   621	            // CC-48 round-9 MEDIUM-HIGH-B3: self-healing. A promoted user the migration
   622	            // seed missed carries stock this ledger has never seen, so the FIRST proposal
   623	            // that touches them books their whole standing above-floor limit. Counted as
   624	            // `backfill` rather than as uplift so it cannot spuriously trip the
   625	            // per-proposal cap, which measures what THIS proposal issued.
   626	            if (creditPopulationEpochOf[user] != marker) {
   627	                creditPopulationEpochOf[user] = marker;
   628	                creditPopulationTotal += 1;
   629	                if (oldLimit > floorLimit) backfill += oldLimit - floorLimit;
   630	            }
   631	            globalReputation[user] = clamped;
   632	            lastReputationEpoch[user] = epoch;
   633	            emit GlobalReputationUpdated(user, clamped, epoch);
   634	            unchecked { ++i; }
   635	        }
   636	        uint256 cap = maxAggregateCreditUpliftPerProposal;
   637	        if (aggregateUplift > cap) revert AggregateCreditUpliftExceeded(aggregateUplift, cap);
   638	
   639	        // CC-48 HIGH-1: the per-proposal cap above is only a transaction-level guard --
   640	        // a colluding quorum can emit N proposals in one block and mint N * cap. The real
   641	        // bound is this running stock of outstanding credit exposure, which every proposal
   642	        // is measured against no matter how the work is sliced.
   643	        //
   644	        // The stock is exact rather than approximate because the ONLY other things that can
   645	        // move a user's credit limit -- `setCreditTier` and `setLevelThresholds` -- do not
   646	        // edit this number, they INVALIDATE it (round-9 HIGH-B1): they shut this path until
   647	        // governance re-counts. So between two re-counts the tier schedule is frozen and
   648	        // these deltas are the whole of the movement.
   649	        //
   650	        // Saturating subtraction keeps the running total from ever underflowing. Round-8
   651	        // established the comparison that matters: the alternative to saturating is
   652	        // REVERTING, which would leave the downgrade unapplied and the user holding the
   653	        // HIGHER limit -- strictly worse for the bound. It can only be more conservative.
   654	        uint256 total = totalCreditExposure + aggregateUplift + backfill;
   655	        total = total > aggregateRelease ? total - aggregateRelease : 0;
   656	        uint256 totalCap = maxTotalCreditExposure;
   657	        if (total > totalCap) revert TotalCreditExposureExceeded(total, totalCap);
   658	        totalCreditExposure = total;
   659	
   660	        emit ReputationProposalUplift(proposalId, aggregateUplift, cap, total, totalCap);
   661	    }
   662	
   663	    function updateOperatorBlacklist(
   664	        address operator,
   665	        address[] calldata users,
   666	        bool[] calldata statuses,
   667	        bytes calldata proof
   668	    ) external nonReentrant {
   669	        if (operator == address(0)) revert InvalidParam();
   670	        if (msg.sender != blsAggregator) revert UnauthorizedSource();
   671	        if (users.length != statuses.length) revert LenMismatch();
   672	        if (users.length > 200) revert BatchTooLarge();
   673	        if (SUPER_PAYMASTER == address(0)) revert SPNotSet();
   674	        if (proof.length == 0) revert BLSProofRequired();
   675	
   676	        uint256 nonce = blacklistNonce + 1;
   677	        blacklistNonce = nonce;
   678	
   679	        _verifyBLS(proof, keccak256(abi.encode(
   680	            blsDomainSeparator(), TAG_BLACKLIST, nonce, operator, users, statuses
   681	        )));
   682	
   683	        ISuperPaymaster(SUPER_PAYMASTER).updateBlockedStatus(operator, users, statuses);
   684	    }
   685	
   686	    /// @notice Emitted when the BLS aggregator marks a proposal id executed.
   687	    /// @dev    L-7: gives off-chain monitors an auditable signal for every
   688	    ///         executedProposals write on this path, so a trusted-but-misbehaving
   689	    ///         aggregator pre-locking a proposal id is observable (the call is
   690	    ///         already gated to `blsAggregator`; this adds visibility, not a gate).
   691	    event ProposalMarkedExecuted(uint256 indexed proposalId, address indexed by);
   692	
   693	    function markProposalExecuted(uint256 proposalId) external {
   694	        if (msg.sender != blsAggregator) revert UnauthorizedSource();
   695	        if (proposalId == 0) revert InvalidProposalId();
   696	        executedProposals[proposalId] = true;
   697	        emit ProposalMarkedExecuted(proposalId, msg.sender);
   698	    }
   699	
   700	    /// @notice Set the aPNT credit limit of one reputation level.
   701	    /// @dev    CC-48 round-9 HIGH-B1. This call changes the credit limit of EVERY user
   702	    ///         sitting at `level`, so it necessarily changes protocol-wide exposure -- and
   703	    ///         round-8 let it do that while `totalCreditExposure` sat still, which is how
   704	    ///         one owner call moved the drawable total 16.6x without the protocol-wide cap
   705	    ///         noticing. It is not allowed to leave the ledger behind any more: the
   706	    ///         population is INVALIDATED in the same call (`_invalidateCreditPopulation`),
   707	    ///         which discards the now-meaningless stock and shuts the reputation path until
   708	    ///         governance re-counts. There is no "remember to re-seed afterwards" step to
   709	    ///         forget, because forgetting it stops issuance rather than un-bounding it.
   710	    ///
   711	    ///         Two writes are exempt, and only because neither can move anybody's credit
   712	    ///         limit: re-writing the price a level already has, and pricing a level above
   713	    ///         the currently reachable top (CC-48 round-11 LOW-L1). See the branches
   714	    ///         below for why each is safe; every write that CAN move a limit invalidates.
   715	    ///
   716	    ///         CC-48 round-9 LOW-B6: `initialize` documents the schedule as monotonic
   717	    ///         ("higher reputation never lowers credit"). That invariant is now ENFORCED
   718	    ///         against the immediate neighbours, which is sufficient by induction because
   719	    ///         every level is written through this function.
   720	    function setCreditTier(uint256 level, uint256 limit) external onlyOwner {
   721	        uint256 maxLevel = levelThresholds.length + 1;
   722	        // 20 thresholds is the hard cap (`setLevelThresholds`), so 21 is the highest level
   723	        // that can ever be reachable. Levels ABOVE the currently reachable top are allowed
   724	        // deliberately: `setLevelThresholds` refuses to grow the schedule onto a level whose
   725	        // price is still 0, so a growth has to price the new top FIRST. The two rules meet
   726	        // in the middle -- a level's price must exist before the level does -- and neither
   727	        // can be satisfied by writing a number nobody checks.
   728	        if (level == 0 || level > 21) revert InvalidParam();
   729	        if (level > 1 && limit < creditTierConfig[level - 1]) revert CreditTiersNotMonotonic();
   730	        if (level < maxLevel && limit > creditTierConfig[level + 1]) revert CreditTiersNotMonotonic();
   731	        // Re-writing the price it already has moves nobody's credit limit, so it must not
   732	        // cost a governance outage. Everything below this line assumes the schedule really
   733	        // changed.
   734	        if (creditTierConfig[level] == limit) return;
   735	        creditTierConfig[level] = limit;
   736	        emit CreditTierUpdated(level, limit);
   737	        // CC-48 round-11 LOW-L1. Same principle as the no-op early return above: a write
   738	        // that moves NOBODY's credit limit must not cost a governance outage. Levels above
   739	        // `maxLevel` are exactly that -- `_levelForReputation` never returns more than
   740	        // `levelThresholds.length + 1`, so no `getCreditLimit`, no proposal delta and no
   741	        // re-count can read this slot while it stays out of reach, and the outstanding
   742	        // stock is therefore still exact. It matters because pricing an unreachable level
   743	        // is the MANDATORY first step of growing the schedule (`setLevelThresholds` refuses
   744	        // to grow onto an unpriced level), so round-9 charged a full re-count for the one
   745	        // move that has to happen first.
   746	        //
   747	        // Nothing escapes through this branch: the step that makes such a level reachable
   748	        // is `setLevelThresholds`, which invalidates unconditionally. The ledger is
   749	        // therefore discarded at the moment the price starts applying to someone, not
   750	        // before.
   751	        if (level <= maxLevel) _invalidateCreditPopulation();
   752	    }
   753	
   754	    /// @dev CC-48 round-9 HIGH-B1. The one place that answers "what happens to the ledger
   755	    ///      when the schedule moves under it". Re-pricing a tier or moving a threshold
   756	    ///      changes the credit limit of users this contract cannot enumerate, so the
   757	    ///      outstanding stock becomes unknowable from storage alone. It is therefore
   758	    ///      DISCARDED, not adjusted, and the reputation path is shut
   759	    ///      (`creditPopulationSeededAt = 0`) until governance re-counts through
   760	    ///      `seedCreditPopulation`, which reads every user's level out of this contract's
   760	    ///      `seedCreditPopulation`, which reads every user's level out of this contract's
   761	    ///      own storage. Bumping the epoch un-counts every address at once -- there is no
   762	    ///      enumerable user set to iterate, and this needs none.
   763	    ///
   764	    ///      The failure mode is a governance outage (no new reputation credit until the
   765	    ///      re-count lands), never a bound that silently stops measuring reality.
   766	    function _invalidateCreditPopulation() internal {
   767	        // Nothing counted means this ledger is tracking nobody, so there is no stock for a
   768	        // schedule change to invalidate and nothing to re-count -- shutting the path would
   769	        // be pure governance damage. That is the state a fresh deployment configures its
   770	        // tier economics in, and the state an upgraded proxy sits in before it is seeded
   771	        // (already shut: `creditPopulationSeededAt` starts at zero and only seeding opens
   772	        // it).
   773	        //
   774	        // Note precisely what this does NOT claim: it does not claim no promoted address
   775	        // exists. An operator who finalized a seed with an incomplete list leaves promoted
   776	        // addresses uncounted, and those keep their real credit limits. They are not lost
   777	        // and they are not silently under-bounded either -- the first proposal that touches
   778	        // one books its whole standing above-floor limit against whatever schedule is in
   779	        // force by then. Uncounted addresses are the backfill's job, not this branch's.
   780	        if (creditPopulationTotal == 0) return;
   781	        creditPopulationEpoch += 1;
   782	        creditPopulationTotal = 0;
   783	        creditPopulationSeededAt = 0;
   784	        totalCreditExposure = 0;
   785	        emit CreditExposureResynced(0);
   786	    }
   787	
   788	    /// @notice Set both credit bounds in one owner call.
   789	    /// @dev    Deliberately a single entry point: the two caps are one policy, and an
   790	    ///         upgrade batch that set them in separate transactions would leave a window
   791	    ///         where the protocol-wide ceiling was still 0 (fail-closed, but a governance
   792	    ///         outage) or still unbounded.
   793	    ///
   794	    ///         CC-48 round-9 MEDIUM-HIGH-B3: the exposure BASELINE is gone from this
   795	    ///         signature. It used to be an operator-supplied aPNT number that nothing
   796	    ///         on-chain could check and that the documented derivation computed WRONG on a
   797	    ///         live deployment. The only thing governance still declares is the population
   798	    ///         MEMBERSHIP -- see `seedCreditPopulation`, where the contract reads each
   799	    ///         member's reputation out of its own storage instead of trusting a total.
   800	    /// @param perProposalCap   Transaction-level guard: max positive credit-limit uplift
   801	    ///                         summed within a single proposal.
   802	    /// @param totalCap         Protocol-wide ceiling on outstanding credit exposure.
   803	    function setCreditPolicy(uint256 perProposalCap, uint256 totalCap) external onlyOwner {
   804	        maxAggregateCreditUpliftPerProposal = perProposalCap;
   805	        maxTotalCreditExposure = totalCap;
   806	        // Refuse a ceiling below the exposure this ledger is currently TRACKING.
   807	        //
   808	        // CC-48 round-11 LOW-L2, stated exactly rather than generously. Round-9 described
   809	        // this line as preventing a frozen reputation path, which overclaims: while the
   810	        // population is invalidated (`creditPopulationSeededAt == 0`, after a
   811	        // `setCreditTier` / `setLevelThresholds` that moved a live schedule)
   812	        // `totalCreditExposure` reads 0 by construction, so this comparison passes for ANY
   813	        // ceiling while real, drawable limits are still outstanding. In that window it
   814	        // guards nothing.
   815	        //
   816	        // It is not the protection, and it was never the only one. The protection is
   817	        // `seedCreditPopulation`, which recomputes exposure from this contract's own
   818	        // storage and reverts the whole batch when the recount exceeds the ceiling -- so a
   819	        // too-low ceiling set in the window cannot open the reputation path, it can only
   820	        // keep it shut until governance raises the ceiling and re-counts. Fail-closed, just
   821	        // reported later. This line's real job is the narrower one it can actually do:
   822	        // catch the mistake immediately in the ordinary, seeded case.
   823	        //
   824	        // Deliberately NOT fixed by refusing to lower the ceiling while unseeded: lowering
   825	        // it is the conservative move, and an outage window is when governance is most
   826	        // likely to need it.
   827	        uint256 exposure = totalCreditExposure;
   828	        if (exposure > totalCap) revert TotalCreditExposureExceeded(exposure, totalCap);
   829	        emit CreditPolicyUpdated(perProposalCap, totalCap, exposure);
   830	    }
   831	
   832	    /// @notice Seed (or re-count) the credit population from this contract's own storage.
   833	    /// @dev    CC-48 round-9 MEDIUM-HIGH-B3. Two situations need it: an UPGRADED proxy,
   834	    ///         whose population slots read empty while promoted users already exist, and a
   835	    ///         schedule change (`setCreditTier` / `setLevelThresholds`), which invalidates
   836	    ///         the count it was computed from. Both leave
   837	    ///         `creditPopulationSeededAt == 0`, which shuts `updateGlobalReputation`, so
   838	    ///         neither can quietly issue credit against a wrong stock.
   839	    ///
   840	    ///         Governance supplies ONLY an address list. Each address's level is read from
   841	    ///         `globalReputation` here, so no operator arithmetic — and in particular no
   842	    ///         repetition of the old, structurally wrong "sum the GlobalReputationUpdated
   843	    ///         events into an aPNT baseline" recipe — can misstate a tier.
   844	    ///         `expectedPopulationTotal` is a declared headcount checked against what the
   845	    ///         contract actually counted, so a truncated batch cannot be finalized.
   846	    ///
   847	    ///         Addresses that were never promoted may be omitted: they sit at level 1 and
   848	    ///         contribute exactly zero to the derived stock (see `totalCreditExposure`).
   849	    ///         A promoted address missed here is self-healing — the next proposal touching
   850	    ///         it counts it at its then-current level.
   851	    ///
   852	    ///         Re-running this after finalization is harmless rather than forbidden: an
   853	    ///         address already counted in the current epoch is skipped, so the only effect
   854	    ///         of a second call is to count addresses that were missed. Refusing it would
   855	    ///         buy nothing and would make the migration batch order-dependent on whether
   856	    ///         the proxy had ever been initialized by this implementation.
   857	    /// @param users                   candidate addresses. Already-counted ones are skipped,
   858	    ///                                so the list may be split into batches and may overlap.
   859	    /// @param expectedPopulationTotal headcount governance declares; checked on finalize.
   860	    /// @param finalize                declare the count complete and re-open the path.
   861	    function seedCreditPopulation(
   862	        address[] calldata users,
   863	        uint256 expectedPopulationTotal,
   864	        bool finalize
   865	    ) external onlyOwner {
   866	        uint256 marker = creditPopulationEpoch + 1;
   867	        uint256 counted = creditPopulationTotal;
   868	        uint256 exposure = totalCreditExposure;
   869	        uint256 floor = creditTierConfig[1];
   870	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < users.length; i++) {
   871	            address user = users[i];
   872	            if (creditPopulationEpochOf[user] == marker) continue;
   873	            creditPopulationEpochOf[user] = marker;
   874	            counted += 1;
   875	            uint256 limit = creditTierConfig[_levelForReputation(globalReputation[user])];
   876	            if (limit > floor) exposure += limit - floor;
   877	        }
   878	        creditPopulationTotal = counted;
   879	        totalCreditExposure = exposure;
   880	        // Checked on EVERY batch, not only the finalizing one: a partial count that already
   881	        // exceeds the ceiling can only get worse, and leaving an over-ceiling number in the
   882	        // slot between batches is a state no later call would re-examine.
   883	        uint256 cap = maxTotalCreditExposure;
   884	        if (exposure > cap) revert TotalCreditExposureExceeded(exposure, cap);
   885	        if (finalize) {
   886	            if (counted != expectedPopulationTotal) revert CreditPopulationCountMismatch();
   887	            creditPopulationSeededAt = block.timestamp;
   888	        }
   889	        emit CreditExposureResynced(exposure);
   890	    }
   891	
   892	    function setReputationSource(address source, bool active) external onlyOwner {
   893	        isReputationSource[source] = active;
   894	        emit ReputationSourceUpdated(source, active);
   895	    }
   896	
   897	    /// @notice Replace the reputation thresholds.
   898	    /// @dev    CC-48 round-9 HIGH-B1. Moving a threshold moves users between levels with no
   899	    ///         proposal involved, so like `setCreditTier` it changes the credit limit of
   900	    ///         users this contract cannot enumerate. Same treatment, for the same reason:
   901	    ///         the population is DISCARDED and the reputation path shuts
   902	    ///         (`creditPopulationSeededAt` back to zero) until governance re-counts through
   903	    ///         `seedCreditPopulation`, which reads every level out of this contract's own
   904	    ///         `globalReputation` storage. Bumping `creditPopulationEpoch` un-counts every
   905	    ///         address at once without needing an enumerable user set.
   906	    ///
   907	    ///         The alternative considered and rejected was accepting an operator-supplied
   908	    ///         re-bucketing (a per-level headcount passed as calldata). It would keep the
   909	    ///         path open across a threshold move, but the numbers in it are exactly the
   910	    ///         kind of unverifiable operator arithmetic round-9 exists to remove.
   911	    ///
   912	    ///         The failure mode is a governance outage (no new credit until the re-count
   913	    ///         lands), never a silent decoupling of the bound from reality.
   914	    function setLevelThresholds(uint256[] calldata thresholds) external onlyOwner {
   915	        if (thresholds.length > 20) revert TooManyLevels();
   916	        delete levelThresholds;
   917	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < thresholds.length; i++) {
   918	            if (i > 0 && thresholds[i] <= thresholds[i - 1]) revert ThreshNotAscending();
   919	            levelThresholds.push(thresholds[i]);
   920	        }
   921	        // CC-48 round-9 LOW-B6, second half. `setCreditTier` cannot make the table
   922	        // non-monotonic, but GROWING the schedule here can: a new top level whose tier was
   923	        // never configured reads 0, so the highest-reputation users would drop to a limit
   924	        // BELOW the level under them -- precisely the "higher reputation never lowers
   925	        // credit" invariant, broken from the other side. Check the resulting table, not
   926	        // just the thresholds.
   927	        uint256 maxLevel = thresholds.length + 1;
   928	        for (uint256 level = 2; level <= maxLevel; level++) {
   929	            if (creditTierConfig[level] < creditTierConfig[level - 1]) revert CreditTiersNotMonotonic();
   930	        }
   931	        _invalidateCreditPopulation();
   932	    }
   933	
   934	    function getCreditLimit(address user) external view returns (uint256) {
   935	        return creditTierConfig[_levelForReputation(globalReputation[user])];
   936	    }
   937	
   938	    function _levelForReputation(uint256 rep) internal view returns (uint256) {
   939	        for (uint256 i = levelThresholds.length; i > 0; i--) {
   940	            if (rep >= levelThresholds[i - 1]) {
   941	                return i + 1;
   942	            }
   943	        }
   944	        return 1;
   945	    }
   946	
   947	    function _validateAndProcessRole(bytes32 roleId, address user, bytes calldata roleData)
   948	        internal returns (uint256 stakeAmount, bytes memory sbtData)
   949	    {
   950	        if (roleId == ROLE_COMMUNITY) {
   951	            CommunityRoleData memory data = abi.decode(roleData, (CommunityRoleData));
   952	            if (bytes(data.name).length == 0) revert InvalidParam();
   953	            if (communityByName[data.name] != address(0)) revert InvalidParam();
   954	            if (bytes(data.ensName).length > 0 && communityByENS[data.ensName] != address(0)) revert InvalidParam();
   955	            stakeAmount = data.stakeAmount;
   956	            sbtData = abi.encode(user, "");
   957	            communityByName[data.name] = user;
   958	            if (bytes(data.ensName).length > 0) communityByENS[data.ensName] = user;
   959	        } else if (roleId == ROLE_ENDUSER) {
   960	            EndUserRoleData memory data = abi.decode(roleData, (EndUserRoleData));
   961	            if (!hasRole[ROLE_COMMUNITY][data.community]) revert InvalidParam();
   962	            stakeAmount = data.stakeAmount;
   963	            sbtData = abi.encode(data.community, "");
   964	        } else {
   965	            if (roleData.length == 32) stakeAmount = abi.decode(roleData, (uint256));
   966	            sbtData = abi.encode(user, "");
   967	        }
   968	        if (SUPER_PAYMASTER != address(0)) {
   969	            // M-2: the REGISTER path stays fatal (fail-closed) on purpose. Registration is atomic
   970	            // (no stake locked yet if it reverts), so a revert here is a clean rollback the user can
   971	            // retry — unlike exitRole, where a fatal call would freeze already-locked stake. Making
   972	            // this non-fatal would risk a silent half-registration (registered but not SBT-eligible).
   973	            ISuperPaymaster(SUPER_PAYMASTER).updateSBTStatus(user, true);
   974	        }
   975	    }
   976	
   977	    function getRoleConfig(bytes32 roleId) external view returns (RoleConfig memory) { return roleConfigs[roleId]; }
   978	    function getUserRoles(address user) external view returns (bytes32[] memory) { return userRoles[user]; }
   979	    function getRoleUserCount(bytes32 roleId) external view returns (uint256) { return roleMembers[roleId].length; }
   980	    function getRoleStake(bytes32 roleId, address user) external view returns (uint256) { return roleStakes[roleId][user]; }
   981	    function getCommunityByName(string calldata name) external view returns (address) { return communityByName[name]; }
   982	    function getCommunityByENS(string calldata ensName) external view returns (address) { return communityByENS[ensName]; }
   983	
   984	    function _removeFromRoleMembers(bytes32 roleId, address user) internal {
   985	        uint256 indexPlusOne = roleMemberIndex[roleId][user];
   986	        if (indexPlusOne == 0) return;
   987	        address[] storage members = roleMembers[roleId];
   988	        uint256 index = indexPlusOne - 1;
   989	        uint256 lastIndex = members.length - 1;
   990	        if (index != lastIndex) {
   991	            address lastUser = members[lastIndex];
   992	            members[index] = lastUser;
   993	            roleMemberIndex[roleId][lastUser] = indexPlusOne;
   994	        }
   995	        members.pop();
   996	        delete roleMemberIndex[roleId][user];
   997	    }
   998	
   999	    function _removeFromUserRoles(address user, bytes32 roleId) internal {
  1000	        bytes32[] storage roles = userRoles[user];
  1001	        uint256 length = roles.length;
  1002	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < length; i++) {
  1003	            if (roles[i] == roleId) {
  1004	                roles[i] = roles[length - 1];
  1005	                roles.pop();
  1006	                break;
  1007	            }
  1008	        }
  1009	    }
  1010	
  1011	    /// @dev GOV-2: no implementation change while a two-step nomination is pending (see Ownable2StepNamespaced).
  1012	    function _authorizeUpgrade(address) internal override onlyOwner { _requireNoPendingOwner(); }
  1013	
  1014	    /// @notice Monotonic nonce for blacklist BLS proofs (P0-3 replay protection).
  1015	    uint256 public blacklistNonce;
  1016	
  1017	    /// @notice Maximum positive credit-limit uplift (aPNT, 18 decimals) per proposal.
  1018	    /// @dev    Transaction-level guard only. It bounds one proposal, NOT total
  1019	    ///         issuance — see totalCreditExposure for the protocol-wide bound.
  1020	    uint256 public maxAggregateCreditUpliftPerProposal;
  1021	
  1022	    error AggregateCreditUpliftExceeded(uint256 aggregateUplift, uint256 cap);
  1023	
  1024	    /// @notice Protocol-wide OUTSTANDING aPNT credit exposure created by the REPUTATION
  1025	    ///         path: the sum, over every address, of the credit limit its current global
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     2	// AAStar.io contribution with love from 2023
     3	pragma solidity 0.8.33;
     4	
     5	import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";
     6	
     7	/**
     8	 * @title Ownable2StepNamespaced
     9	 * @notice GOV-2 (spec 03 §10.7b, "GOV-2 规范（第 3 版）" A/B): two-step ownership transfer for the
    10	 *         UUPS proxies SuperPaymaster and Registry, WITHOUT touching their sequential storage.
    11	 * @dev    Both proxies inherit OZ v5.0.2's non-upgradeable `Ownable`: `_owner` is sequential slot 0
    12	 *         and the next contract's state starts at slot 1. OZ `Ownable2Step` would insert
    13	 *         `_pendingOwner` right after `_owner` and shift every later slot — FORBIDDEN (spec A).
    14	 *         The pending owner therefore lives in an ERC-7201 namespaced slot:
    15	 *           keccak256(abi.encode(uint256(keccak256("aastar.storage.Ownership2Step")) - 1)) & ~bytes32(uint256(0xff))
    16	 *         This contract declares NO state variable, so the inheriting contract's sequential layout
    17	 *         is byte-identical to the `Ownable` one (checked by scripts/check_storage_layout.py).
    18	 *
    19	 *         Behaviour (spec B.1–B.4):
    20	 *           - transferOwnership(newOwner): owner only; records the nomination and emits
    21	 *             OwnershipTransferStarted. newOwner == address(0) cancels. A second call replaces it.
    22	 *           - acceptOwnership(): only the pending owner.
    23	 *           - _transferOwnership: clears the nomination BEFORE changing the owner, so every path
    24	 *             that changes the owner (accept, initialize, a future reinitializer) voids a stale
    25	 *             nomination.
    26	 *           - renounceOwnership(): always reverts.
    27	 *
    28	 *         SELECTOR SHADOWING (D5b-design §2.1): SuperPaymaster reaches its admin extension through
    29	 *         fallback DELEGATECALL; a selector the core has is ALWAYS answered by the core. These
    30	 *         overrides must therefore live in the core's inheritance chain (here), never only in the
    31	 *         extension — otherwise the inherited single-step OZ `transferOwnership` would stay live.
    32	 */
    33	abstract contract Ownable2StepNamespaced is Ownable {
    34	    /// @dev ERC-7201 slot of the pending owner (see contract NatSpec for the derivation).
    35	    bytes32 internal constant OWNERSHIP_2STEP_SLOT =
    36	        0xdb5a3168abaa6147a9f3a4cb66016161119d4d50b6393344d27120286f742a00;
    37	
    38	    event OwnershipTransferStarted(address indexed previousOwner, address indexed newOwner);
    39	
    40	    /// @notice renounceOwnership is disabled (GOV-2 B.4): an ownerless proxy can never be upgraded.
    41	    error OwnershipRenounceDisabled();
    42	
    43	    /// @notice An implementation change was attempted while a nomination is pending.
    44	    error PendingOwnershipTransfer(address pendingOwner);
    45	
    46	    /// @dev Called from `_authorizeUpgrade`. A rollback to a pre-GOV-2 implementation (single-step
    47	    ///      OZ Ownable, which neither reads nor clears the ERC-7201 slot) would otherwise let a stale
    48	    ///      nomination survive an owner change made under that implementation and be accepted after
    49	    ///      the next forward upgrade (Codex D5b review, High). Cancel with transferOwnership(address(0))
    50	    ///      before upgrading.
    51	    function _requireNoPendingOwner() internal view {
    52	        address p = pendingOwner();
    53	        if (p != address(0)) revert PendingOwnershipTransfer(p);
    54	    }
    55	
    56	    /// @notice The nominated owner; address(0) when there is no pending transfer.
    57	    function pendingOwner() public view virtual returns (address p) {
    58	        bytes32 slot = OWNERSHIP_2STEP_SLOT;
    59	        assembly ("memory-safe") { p := sload(slot) }
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    /// @notice Start (or replace, or with address(0) cancel) a two-step ownership transfer.
    63	    /// @dev    `onlyOwner` is written out explicitly: an override does NOT inherit modifiers.
    64	    function transferOwnership(address newOwner) public virtual override onlyOwner {
    65	        _setPendingOwner(newOwner);
    66	        emit OwnershipTransferStarted(owner(), newOwner);
    67	    }
    68	
    69	    /// @notice Complete a transfer started by `transferOwnership`; callable only by the nominee.
    70	    function acceptOwnership() public virtual {
    71	        address sender = _msgSender();
    72	        if (pendingOwner() != sender) revert OwnableUnauthorizedAccount(sender);
    73	        _transferOwnership(sender);
    74	    }
    75	
    76	    /// @notice Always reverts (GOV-2 B.4).
    77	    function renounceOwnership() public virtual override {
    78	        revert OwnershipRenounceDisabled();
    79	    }
    80	
    81	    /// @dev Clears the nomination first, then changes the owner (GOV-2 B.3).
    82	    function _transferOwnership(address newOwner) internal virtual override {
    83	        _setPendingOwner(address(0));
    84	        super._transferOwnership(newOwner);
    85	    }
    86	
    87	    function _setPendingOwner(address p) private {
    88	        bytes32 slot = OWNERSHIP_2STEP_SLOT;
    89	        assembly ("memory-safe") { sstore(slot, p) }
    90	    }
    91	}

     1	# rc.8 Daybreak fix set — 2026-11-09
     2	
     3	Branch `cc125/rc8-daybreak-fixes`.
     4	Input: `C4-report.md`, `C2-report.md`, `C1-report.md` in
     5	the Daybreak review archive (2026-11-09, held by the maintainer and DSR).
     6	The initial `git diff 8de39026 -- contracts/src` was empty. No network, chain,
     7	key access, commits, or Git metadata writes were performed.
     8	The pre-existing OpenZeppelin v5.1.0 `package-lock.json` edit was preserved.
     9	
    10	## Implementation
    11	
    12	### C4-H-01: separate signing cutoff and withdrawal readiness
    13	
    14	`BLSAggregator-4.14.0` retains the two-day public notice. For request time `t`:
    15	
    16	- `readyAt = t + 2 days`: signing stops, exactly as before.
    17	- `withdrawAt = readyAt + GUARDIAN_FRAUD_CHALLENGE = t + 3 days`.
    18	- `expiresAt = withdrawAt + GUARDIAN_EXIT_WINDOW = t + 4 days`.
    19	- Withdrawal is permitted at both endpoints `[withdrawAt, expiresAt]`, absent a
    20	  pending case. `GuardianExitNotReady` now reports `withdrawAt`.
    21	- `GUARDIAN_SLASH_CASE_WINDOW = 5 days > 2 + 1 + 1 days`.
    22	  `VERIFIER_ROTATION_DELAY` remains tied to that constant and becomes five days.
    23	
    24	Every other use of the notice fields was checked:
    25	
    26	- `_reconstructPkAgg` is unchanged: a nonzero notice excludes its key from
    27	  `readyAt`, including throughout the challenge period and after `expiresAt`.
    28	  Expiry never silently restores a potentially fraudulent signer.
    29	- `_requireCommitteeSurvivesExit` already excludes all other announced exits,
    30	  even before their cutoff. It conservatively checks the eventual signer set;
    31	  delaying withdrawal does not change the set or weaken the quorum floor.
    32	- Cancellation deletes the notice, restores signing eligibility, and starts the
    33	  existing one-day cooldown. Pending cases still prohibit cancellation and new
    34	  notices. A cancelled notice cannot be consumed; a fresh notice incurs the full
    35	  notice and challenge again. Expired notices still require cancellation and
    36	  cooldown before replacement.
    37	- `consumeGuardianExit` still checks pending cases before timing, so a case queued
    38	  during the challenge freezes the guardian even after withdrawal would mature.
    39	  Successful consumption deletes the notice atomically with Registry role exit.
    40	- Any notice already open when a case is queued expires before that case's
    41	  five-day deadline. New notices are forbidden during a pending case. The old
    42	  comment suggesting an accused guardian could open a notice after queueing was
    43	  corrected rather than retained.
    44	- Fraud-verifier rotation retains its full-case-window delay and verdict pinning.
    45	  Request structs, event fields and storage layout are unchanged; the public
    46	  `readyAt` event/getter field still means signing cutoff, not withdrawal time.
    47	
    48	Regression coverage includes a real Prague BLS signature executed at `readyAt-1`,
    49	Registry exits rejected at `readyAt` and the last challenge second, a fraud case
    50	queued during the challenge and enforced after maturity, and a subsequent actual
    51	stake slash. EVM-independent Registry/staking tests cover clean withdrawal at
    52	exact maturity, withdrawal at exact new expiry, and rejection one second later.
    53	Existing cutoff, cancellation, cooldown, expired-key exclusion and committee-floor
    54	tests remain in place; existing withdrawal/expiry timings were updated.
    55	
    56	### C4-L-01 and C4-L-02 fixed in rc.8 (BLSAggregator-4.14.0)
    57	
    58	C4-L-02: generic `executeProposal` stores the signer commitment after signature verification and before
    59	calling the arbitrary target (test_GenericCommitmentIsStoredBeforeTargetCall,
    60	test_Prague_GenericProposalRecordsSigners).
    61	
    62	C4-L-01: generic and slash proposals no longer share one id space. This supersedes the 11-09 note that
    63	deferred C4-L-01 to 5.5.1; DSR then ruled it into rc.8, together with the typed keys and
    64	MAX_VALIDATORS = 64 (commit "typed replay/commitment keys, anchored signer commitments, MAX_VALIDATORS 64").
    65	- The generic path keys replay protection and its commitment by
    66	  `uint256(keccak256(abi.encode(TAG_PROPOSAL, proposalId)))`. The `markProposalExecuted` callback to
    67	  DVTValidator is removed, so a generic id can no longer pre-consume a slash id that DVTValidator allocates
    68	  later.
    69	- Raw slash and reputation ids must be below 2^128 (`InvalidProposalId`), which separates them from the
    70	  keccak-derived generic and queue keys by 128 bits.
    71	- Each path stores its commitment under its own key, and the commitment also binds
    72	  `blockhash(block.number - 1)`. Every write emits `SignersCommitted(key, signerMask)`.
    73	- MAX_VALIDATORS is 64, and slot 65 reverts with `SlotOutOfRange`.
    74	
    75	Tests that run on Prague with genuine keys:
    76	- both paths keep both commitments: test_QueueSlashGeneric_OverlappingInputsKeepAllCommitments;
    77	- the crafted collision: test_SlashRawIdEqualToGenericKeyCannotOverwriteGenericCommitment;
    78	- slot 65: test_RegisterBLSPublicKey_SlotAboveMax_Reverts, test_Verify_RejectsBitsBeyondMaxValidators;
    79	- the typed key per path in the recompute: test_QueueSlashGeneric_OverlappingInputsKeepAllCommitments,
    80	  test_GenericCommitmentIsStoredBeforeTargetCall.
    81	
    82	Version: `BLSAggregator-4.14.0`. Main had already carried `4.13.0` for the build with C4-L-01 deferred.
    83	That build was never deployed, but two bytecodes must not share a version.
    84	
    85	### C2-01: invalidate stale standby roles
    86	
    87	`_setCurrentSP` clears a matching designated standby and emits `StandbyInvalidated`.
    88	It separately clears a matching pending standby **and ETA**, emitting
    89	`StandbyProposalCancelled`. No storage was added. Designation activation rechecks
    90	both current SP and emergency-revoked SP after the timelock and before designation.
    91	Tests cover normal promotion of a designated standby, simultaneous mature SP and
    92	standby proposals, cancellation events/ETA clearing, and both activation guards.
    93	The latter seed stale state explicitly, since normal promotion now clears it.
    94	
    95	### C1-L-01: report the applied slash penalty
    96	
    97	`_slash` returns its final capped/clamped penalty. `executeSlashWithBLS` emits that
    98	value in `SlashExecutedWithProof`. The regression deposits 1,000 aPNTs and checks
    99	that a MAJOR slash emits 300, leaves 700, credits 300 revenue and records 300 in
   100	history. SuperPaymaster core/Admin storage is untouched.
   101	
   102	### Registry ENDUSER membership cleanup
   103	
   104	`MySBT.deactivateAllMemberships` cannot attribute a membership to the role that
   105	created it: it iterates every membership stored for the user's SBT and deactivates
   106	every active entry. Therefore `Registry.exitRole(ROLE_ENDUSER)` calls it only when
   107	`userRoleCount[user] == 0` after role removal. If another role remains, all
   108	memberships remain intact. In particular, a per-community membership can outlive
   109	the ENDUSER role while another role is held. Integrators must not treat
   110	`MySBT.verifyCommunityMembership(user, community)` as sufficient authorization;
   111	they must also require `Registry.hasRole(ROLE_ENDUSER, user)`.
   112	
   113	A later final-role exit burns the SBT and deactivates its memberships through
   114	`MySBT.burnSBT`. If a final non-ENDUSER exit occurs while MySBT is paused, the burn
   115	fails non-fatally and sets `pendingSBTBurn`; after unpausing MySBT, anyone can call
   116	`reconcileSBTBurn(user)`. The permissionless
   117	`reconcileMembershipDeactivation` retry applies the same zero-role condition, so
   118	a stale pending flag cannot deactivate memberships while any role remains. If a
   119	third party adds COMMUNITY, ANODE, DVT, KMS, PAYMASTER_AOA, or PAYMASTER_SUPER
   120	(e.g. via a community's `safeMintForRole`) before that retry, recovery requires
   121	the holder to exit every remaining role. COMMUNITY has a zero default lock; the
   122	operator roles use `block.timestamp >= lockedAt + 30 days` (and DVT also requires
   123	its guardian-exit authorization). Exit and reconciliation are separate
   124	transactions, so a sponsor that keeps funding grants can re-grant a role after
   125	each exit and before the retry, delaying recovery indefinitely while it keeps
   810	  `SuperPaymasterStorage._isSlashPending` expires only a guardian queue after
   811	  `GUARDIAN_SLASH_FREEZE`. The owner can always cancel the flag with
   812	  `SuperPaymasterAdmin.cancelSlash`. `usedSlashQueueHashes` makes the proof
   813	  one-shot per `(operator, level, epoch)`, even after cancellation. The pre-flag
   814	  moves no funds: execution through the current DVT requires a separate fresh
   815	  slash proof with an id at or above the current floor. Relabelling an old proof
   816	  with a fresh id also fails signature verification. Owner-direct execution with
   817	  caller-chosen ids remains the residual described above. The independent owner
   818	  slash authority is unchanged.
   819	
   820	`RotationFloorExecution.t.sol` covers stale current-DVT execution after reactivation,
   821	fresh execution, later rotation invalidation, stale queue versus DVT fund-moving execution,
   822	exact namespace exhaustion, suspended key ownership and the disclosed queue
   823	residual. Signature-dependent tests execute with genuine Prague precompiles and
   824	skip on Cancun. `test_RotationAllocationFloorPreservesAuthorizedRegistryProof`
   825	also checks that the same stale reputation proof fails through the reactivated DVT
   826	before succeeding through the authorized Registry source. The same real-Registry
   827	suite adds `test_PostRotationDvtReputationUpdateExecutes` and
   828	`test_OwnerDirectLowIdStillExecutesAfterRotation`, proving successful reputation
   829	updates with genuine signatures on both authorized routes. Final suite counts,
   830	runtime size and changed files are recorded in `.rv-narrow.md`; ABI/docs
   831	regeneration is left to the calling session.
   832	
   833	## Generic-path selector gaps and the staking slasher role (2026-11-15)
   834	
   835	BLSAggregator-4.14.0 as merged in #630 had two gaps on the GENERIC `executeProposal` path
   836	(caller-chosen threshold >= `minThreshold`, no `proposalFloor`). Both are closed here.
   837	
   838	1. **`Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation` was not blocked.** Registry re-verifies the proof
   839	   itself, but under `blsDomainSeparator() = keccak(DOMAIN_NAME, chainid, blsAggregator, Registry)`.
   840	   That domain does not change on a DVT rotation, and Registry has no floor. A reputation proof
   841	   signed before a rotation and never executed could therefore still execute through a generic call,
   842	   bypassing the M1 floor guarantee.
   843	   - The #630 review recorded this path only as "consumes a raw id". The floor bypass was found
   844	     afterwards.
   845	   - Test: `test_PreRotationReputationProofCannotExecuteThroughGenericPath` (Prague, genuine keys).
   846	     Removing the block turns it red.
   847	2. **`GTokenStaking.slash` and `slashByDVT` were not blocked.** `DeployLive` made the aggregator an
   848	   authorized slasher, so a `minThreshold` quorum could slash any stake by any amount. This bypassed
   849	   severity bps, cooldown and the floor.
   850	   - The live Sepolia 4.11.0 aggregator has the same gap, with `minThreshold` 2. Only its owner (the
   851	     deployer EOA) can call `executeProposal` there.
   852	
   853	**Fixes**
   854	- The H-1 denylist now also blocks `batchUpdateGlobalReputation`, `slash` and `slashByDVT`.
   855	  BLSAggregator is 24,529 B (47 B headroom). Per-selector blocking is close to full: 5.5.x should
   856	  move to a per-target allowlist, or move the gates into the callees.
   857	- Versions are BLSAggregator-4.14.1 and DVTValidator-0.8.1. The 4.14.0 / 0.8.0 merged in #630
   858	  carries the gaps above.
   859	- `scripts/check_generic_selector_coverage.py` (CI, every PR) enumerates every non-view function in
   860	  the SuperPaymaster, Registry, GTokenStaking and DVTValidator ABIs. Each function whose code
   861	  references an aggregator identity must be classified as blocked or allowed-with-reason.
   862	- **No slasher role in 5.5.0.** The aggregator's only stake slash is `executeGuardianSlash`, which
   863	  cannot be reached while `fraudProofVerifier == 0`. `DeployLive` / `DeployAnvil` no longer grant the
   864	  role and assert it is absent.
   865	  - Enabling guardian slash later grants the role in the same timelock batch as
   866	    `proposeFraudProofVerifier`.
   867	  - Before enabling, see YetAnotherAA-Validator#441: the verifier must recompute the 4.14.0 signers
   868	    commitment, which includes `blockhash(block.number-1)`.
   869	- **Review L1.** Owner-forwarded `DVTValidator.registerBLSPublicKey` now requires
   870	  `aggregator.owner() == msg.sender`, as `removeValidator` already did.
   871	  - Test: `test_RegisterBLSPublicKey_OwnerDivergenceClosesOwnerPath`.
   872	
   873	**Disclosed residuals**
   874	- `Registry.updateOperatorBlacklist` stays generic-callable. A pre-rotation proof for the next unused
   875	  `blacklistNonce` remains valid. Its effect is limited to blocking or unblocking users; it moves no
   876	  funds.
   877	- `SP.updatePriceDVT` stays generic-callable. Each update is limited to +-20% against the SP cache.
   878	  The +-20% Chainlink band applies only while Chainlink is fresh (< 2 h). With a stale feed, cumulative
   879	  drift is bounded only by `[MIN, MAX]_ETH_USD_PRICE`, freshness and increasing `updatedAt`.
   880	- `ReputationSystem.setCommunityReputation` stays generic-callable (#631 review L1). It is open to
   881	  any Registry reputation source, and the aggregator is one on both chains. The current quorum can
   882	  therefore write any community score at `minThreshold`, with no proof.
   883	  - `communityReputations` has no reader in `contracts/src`. The coverage script fails if one is
   884	    added; that is the reopen condition.
   885	  - The SDK exposes the value only as a raw getter, documented as unused by the protocol.
   886	- The generic path can call `transfer` / `approve` on any token the aggregator holds. The aggregator
   887	  must hold no assets; step 13 reads its GToken, aPNTs, xPNTs and ETH balances back as 0.
   888	- Every "onlyOwner; the aggregator is not the owner" allowance holds only while the aggregator owns
   889	  nothing and holds no role. This is enforced by the coverage script's deploy-path ownership check
   890	  and by the step-13 readbacks OWNER-NOT-AGGREGATOR and AGG-NOT-DVT-VALIDATOR.

codex
The first pass confirms the pinned commit and highlights the main trust boundaries: role owners can rewrite complete role rows; reputation writes require both an authorized caller and Registry-local BLS verification; blacklist calls are aggregator-only and bind a monotonically incremented Registry nonce.      2	// AAStar.io contribution with love from 2023
     3	pragma solidity 0.8.33;
     4	import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";
     5	import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/token/ERC721/IERC721.sol";

    10	/**
    11	 * @title ReputationSystem
    12	 * @notice Advanced reputation calculation and management for the Mycelium Ecosystem.
    13	 * @dev Decoupled scoring logic from storage (Registry V3 holds the final scores).
    14	 */
    15	contract ReputationSystem is Ownable, IReputationCalculator {
    16	    struct Rule {
    17	        uint256 baseScore;
    18	        uint256 activityBonus;
    19	        uint256 maxBonus;
    20	        string description;
    21	    }
    22	
    23	    IRegistry public immutable REGISTRY;
    24	
    25	    // community => ruleId => Rule
    26	    mapping(address => mapping(bytes32 => Rule)) public communityRules;
    27	    // community => list of active rule IDs
    28	    mapping(address => bytes32[]) public communityActiveRules;
    29	
    30	    Rule public defaultRule;
    31	
    32	    // Entropy Factor: community => factor (scaled by 1e18, 1.0 = baseline)
    33	    mapping(address => uint256) public entropyFactors;
    34	
    35	    // Future Extensibility: Explicit storage for community sub-reputation
    36	    // community => user => score
    37	    mapping(address => mapping(address => uint256)) public communityReputations;
    38	
    39	    // NFT Boosts: collection address => bonus points
    40	    mapping(address => uint256) public nftCollectionBoost;
    41	    mapping(address => mapping(address => uint256)) public nftHoldStart; // user => collection => timestamp
    42	    address[] public boostedCollections;
    43	    mapping(address => mapping(bytes32 => bool)) private _ruleListed;
    44	    mapping(address => bool) private _collectionListed;
    45	    mapping(address => mapping(bytes32 => bool)) private _ruleActive;
    46	    uint256 public constant MAX_BOOSTED_COLLECTIONS = 50;
    47	    // One-based position in communityActiveRules; zero means absent. Append-only storage.
    48	    mapping(address => mapping(bytes32 => uint256)) private _activeRuleIndex;
    49	
    50	    error Unauthorized();
    51	    error NotAuthorized();
    52	    error MaxBoostedReached();
    53	    error DoesNotHoldNFT();
    54	    error StillHoldsNFT();
    55	    error InvalidCollection();
    56	    error InvalidAddress();
    57	    error InvalidInput();
    58	
    59	    event RuleUpdated(address indexed community, bytes32 indexed ruleId, uint256 base, uint256 bonus);
    60	    event EntropyFactorUpdated(address indexed community, uint256 factor);
    61	    event CommunityReputationUpdated(address indexed community, address indexed user, uint256 score);
    62	    event ReputationComputed(address indexed user, uint256 score);
    63	    event NFTBoostAdded(address indexed collection, uint256 boost);
    64	    event NFTHoldStartReset(address indexed user, address indexed collection);
    65	
    66	    constructor(address _registry) Ownable(msg.sender) {
    67	        REGISTRY = IRegistry(_registry);
    68	        defaultRule = Rule(10, 1, 100, "Default");
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    function version() external pure override returns (string memory) {
    72	        return "Reputation-0.6.0";
    73	    }
    74	
    75	    /**
    76	     * @notice Governance sets the Entropy Factor for a community.
    77	     * @dev 1e18 = 1.0. Lower factor increases "resistance" to reputation gain.
    78	     */
    79	    function setEntropyFactor(address community, uint256 factor) external onlyOwner {
    80	        entropyFactors[community] = factor;
    81	        emit EntropyFactorUpdated(community, factor);
    82	    }
    83	
    84	    /**
    85	     * @notice Set specific community reputation score (called by DVT/Trusted Source)
    86	     * @dev Allows off-chain calculation results to be stored on-chain for specific communities.
    87	     *      Authorized callers: contract owner OR addresses whitelisted via Registry.isReputationSource().
    88	     */
    89	    function setCommunityReputation(address community, address user, uint256 score) external {
    90	        // Reuse Registry's reputation source whitelist for access control
    91	        if (msg.sender != owner() && !REGISTRY.isReputationSource(msg.sender)) revert Unauthorized();
    92	        communityReputations[community][user] = score;
    93	        emit CommunityReputationUpdated(community, user, score);
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    /**
    97	     * @notice Community admins can set their own scoring rules.
    98	     * @dev Restricted to the owner of the community role in the Registry.
    99	     */
   100	    function setRule(bytes32 ruleId, uint256 base, uint256 bonus, uint256 max, string calldata desc) external {
   101	        address community = msg.sender;
   102	        // Verify msg.sender has the COMMUNITY role (multi-tenant: each community manages its own rules)
   103	        if (!REGISTRY.hasRole(keccak256("COMMUNITY"), msg.sender) && owner() != msg.sender) revert NotAuthorized();
   104	        // Bounds constrain rule parameters, not arbitrary activity inputs or entropy factors;
   105	        // multiplication of those inputs may still overflow and revert in computeScore.
   106	        // base/bonus capped at 10_000 points per rule; max capped at 100_000 (max cumulative bonus).
   107	        if (base > 10_000 || bonus > 10_000 || max > 100_000) revert InvalidInput();
   108	
   109	        _ruleListed[community][ruleId] = true;
   110	        bytes32[] storage activeRules = communityActiveRules[community];
   111	        uint256 index = _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId];
   112	        if (base > 0 && index == 0) {
   113	            activeRules.push(ruleId);
   114	            _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId] = activeRules.length;
   115	        } else if (base == 0 && index != 0) {
   116	            uint256 last = activeRules.length;
   117	            if (index != last) {
   118	                bytes32 moved = activeRules[last - 1];
   119	                activeRules[index - 1] = moved;
   120	                _activeRuleIndex[community][moved] = index;
   121	            }
   122	            activeRules.pop();
   123	            delete _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId];
   124	        }
   125	        _ruleActive[community][ruleId] = base > 0;
   126	        communityRules[community][ruleId] = Rule(base, bonus, max, desc);
   127	        emit RuleUpdated(community, ruleId, base, bonus);
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    function setNFTBoost(address collection, uint256 boost) external onlyOwner {
   131	        if (collection == address(0)) revert InvalidAddress();
   132	        if (!_collectionListed[collection]) {
   133	            if (boostedCollections.length >= MAX_BOOSTED_COLLECTIONS) revert MaxBoostedReached();
   134	            _collectionListed[collection] = true;
   135	            boostedCollections.push(collection);
   136	        }
   137	        nftCollectionBoost[collection] = boost;
   138	        emit NFTBoostAdded(collection, boost);
   139	    }
   140	
   141	    /**
   142	     * @notice Compute reputation for a user based on their community activities.
   143	     * @dev Activities are now mapped to rules.
   144	     */
   145	    function computeScore(
   146	        address user,
   147	        address[] memory communities,
   148	        bytes32[][] memory ruleIds,
   149	        uint256[][] memory activities
   150	    ) public view returns (uint256 totalScore) {
   151	        if (ruleIds.length != communities.length || activities.length != communities.length) revert InvalidInput();
   152	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < communities.length; i++) {
   153	            if (ruleIds[i].length != activities[i].length) revert InvalidInput();
   154	            address community = communities[i];
   155	            uint256 communityScore = 0;
   156	
   157	            for (uint256 j = 0; j < ruleIds[i].length; j++) {
   158	                bytes32 rId = ruleIds[i][j];
   159	                Rule memory r = communityRules[community][rId];
   160	
   161	                // A configured zero-base rule is disabled, not missing.
   162	                if (_ruleListed[community][rId] && !_ruleActive[community][rId]) continue;
   163	
   164	                // If rule was never configured, use the default for the first rule.
   165	                // P1-26 resolved: fallback uses constructor-initialized defaultRule; no setter changes it
   166	                if (!_ruleListed[community][rId] && j == 0) {
   167	                    r = defaultRule;
   168	                }
   169	
   170	                uint256 bonus = activities[i][j] * r.activityBonus;
   171	                if (bonus > r.maxBonus) bonus = r.maxBonus;
   172	                communityScore += r.baseScore + bonus;
   173	            }
   174	
   175	            // Apply Entropy Factor (1e18 = 1.0)
   176	            uint256 factor = entropyFactors[community];
   177	            if (factor == 0) factor = 1e18; // Default to 1.0
   178	
   179	            totalScore += (communityScore * factor) / 1e18;
   180	        }
   181	
   182	        // Global NFT Boosts
   183	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < boostedCollections.length; i++) {
   184	            address collection = boostedCollections[i];
   185	            if (_nftBoostEligible(user, collection)) totalScore += nftCollectionBoost[collection];
   186	        }
   187	    }
   188	
   189	    /**
   190	     * @notice Sync computed reputation score to the Registry for the given user.
   191	     * @dev Computes score via computeScore then calls Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation.
   192	     *      The proposalId is derived deterministically from (user, epoch) to ensure uniqueness.
   193	     */
   194	    function syncToRegistry(
   195	        address user,
   196	        address[] memory communities,
   197	        bytes32[][] memory ruleIds,
   198	        uint256[][] memory activities,
   199	        uint256 epoch,
   200	        bytes calldata proof
   201	    ) external {
   202	        uint256 score = computeScore(user, communities, ruleIds, activities);
   203	        address[] memory users = new address[](1);
   204	        users[0] = user;
   205	        uint256[] memory scores = new uint256[](1);
   206	        scores[0] = score;
   207	
   208	        // Hash-derived ID for (user, epoch). Separation from arbitrary external IDs
   209	        // and nonzero output are probabilistic, not structurally guaranteed.
   210	        // Registry independently verifies the full-width ID and enforces user epochs.
   211	        uint256 proposalId = uint256(keccak256(abi.encode("RS", user, epoch)));
   212	        REGISTRY.batchUpdateGlobalReputation(proposalId, users, scores, epoch, proof);
   213	        emit ReputationComputed(user, score);
   214	    }
   215	
   216	    /**
   217	     * @notice IReputationCalculator implementation for MySBT v2.1+
   218	     */
   219	    function calculateReputation(
   220	        address user,
   221	        address community,
   222	        uint256 /* sbtTokenId */
   223	    )
   224	        external
   225	        view
   226	        override
   227	        returns (uint256 communityScore, uint256 globalScore)
   228	    {
   229	        // Find community rule
   230	        bytes32[] memory rules = communityActiveRules[community];
   231	        bytes32[][] memory ruleIds = new bytes32[][](1);
   232	        ruleIds[0] = rules;
   233	
   234	        uint256[][] memory activities = new uint256[][](1);
   235	        activities[0] = new uint256[](rules.length); // No activity data: every allocated entry is zero
   236	
   237	        address[] memory communities = new address[](1);
   238	        communities[0] = community;
   239	
   240	        communityScore = computeScore(user, communities, ruleIds, activities);
   241	        globalScore = communityScore; // Simplification for now
   242	    }
   243	
   244	    function getReputationBreakdown(
   245	        address user,
   246	        address community,
   247	        uint256 /* sbtTokenId */
   248	    )
   249	        external
   250	        view
   251	        override
   252	        returns (uint256 baseScore, uint256 nftBonus, uint256 activityBonus, uint256 multiplier)
   253	    {
   254	        // Mirror calculateReputation: all active rules, zero activity inputs,
   255	        // then the community multiplier and global NFT boosts.
   256	        bytes32[] memory rules = communityActiveRules[community];
   257	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < rules.length; i++) {
   258	            baseScore += communityRules[community][rules[i]].baseScore;
   259	        }
   260	
   261	        multiplier = entropyFactors[community];
   262	        if (multiplier == 0) multiplier = 1e18;
   263	        baseScore = (baseScore * multiplier) / 1e18;
   264	        activityBonus = 0;
   265	
   266	        // Apply the same registration-age and current-ownership predicate as computeScore.
   267	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < boostedCollections.length; i++) {
   268	            address collection = boostedCollections[i];
   269	            if (_nftBoostEligible(user, collection)) nftBonus += nftCollectionBoost[collection];
   270	        }
   271	    }
   272	
   273	    /// @dev Eligibility is registration age plus current ownership. Strict
   274	    ///      continuity cannot be proven without hooks from third-party NFTs.
   275	    function _nftBoostEligible(address user, address collection) internal view returns (bool) {
   276	        uint256 registeredAt = nftHoldStart[user][collection];
   277	        if (registeredAt == 0 || block.timestamp < registeredAt + 7 days) return false;
   278	        try IERC721(collection).balanceOf(user) returns (uint256 balance) {
   279	            return balance > 0;
   280	        } catch {
   281	            return false;
   282	        }
   283	    }
   284	
   285	    /**
   286	     * @notice Get all active rule IDs for a community
   287	     * @param community The community address
   288	     * @return ruleIds Array of active rule identifiers; order may change on removal
   289	     */
   290	    function getActiveRules(address community) external view returns (bytes32[] memory ruleIds) {
   291	        return communityActiveRules[community];
   292	    }
   293	
   294	    /**
   295	     * @notice Register the waiting period for an NFT reputation boost.
   296	     * @dev Eligibility starts seven days later and also requires current ownership.
   297	     *      This records registration age, not continuous ownership history.
   298	     */
   299	    function updateNFTHoldStart(address collection) external {
   300	        try IERC721(collection).balanceOf(msg.sender) returns (uint256 balance) {
contracts/src/modules/reputation/ReputationSystem.sol:15:contract ReputationSystem is Ownable, IReputationCalculator {
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     2	// AAStar.io contribution with love from 2023
     3	pragma solidity 0.8.33;
     4	import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";
     5	import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/utils/ReentrancyGuard.sol";
     6	import "src/interfaces/v3/IRegistry.sol";
     7	import "src/interfaces/v3/IGTokenStaking.sol";
     8	import "src/interfaces/IVersioned.sol";
     9	import {BLS} from "src/utils/BLS.sol";
    10	
    11	/// @notice Local sub-view of Registry used to fetch the staking pointer at
    12	///         verification time. We cast `REGISTRY` to this narrower interface
    13	///         rather than baking another constructor arg, so existing deploy
    14	///         scripts (4 in production + multiple archives) keep their 3-arg
    15	///         BLSAggregator construction unchanged. Mocks in the test suite
    16	///         already implement this view (set via `setStakingAddr`).
    17	interface IRegistryStakingAwareBLS {

    38	/// @notice Narrow sub-view of GTokenStaking exposing only the directed
    39	///         role-lock slash. `IGTokenStaking` (used elsewhere here for

    41	///         staking pointer to this local interface rather than widening the
    42	///         shared interface (which every mock/implementer would then have to
    43	///         satisfy). The 5.5.0 deploy paths do NOT put the aggregator in
    44	///         `authorizedSlashers` (guardian slash is off while fraudProofVerifier == 0);
    45	///         enabling it grants the role in the same timelock batch as proposeFraudProofVerifier.
