    49	
    50	/// @notice External, DVT-supplied fraud-proof verifier (Protocol B stage 2).
    51	///         Returns true iff `fraudProofId` proves the referenced proposal was
    52	///         fraudulent AND `guiltyGuardians` are exactly the co-signers to blame.
    53	///         Guardians are identified by ADDRESS, not slot: slots are reassignable
    54	///         (revokeBLSPublicKey frees a slot for reuse), so a slot captured at
    55	///         fraud time could later resolve to an innocent validator. Address
    56	///         binding is stable and is what the slash reads.
    57	///         BLSAggregator treats this as the sole authority on "who colluded";
    58	///         it does not itself judge fraud. Kept behind an interface so the
    59	///         detection layer can evolve without touching this contract.
    60	///
    61	///         CC-48 round-2: `domainDigest` is supplied by the aggregator and equals
    62	///         `BLSAggregator.fraudProofDigest(fraudProofId, guiltyGuardians)` =
    63	///         keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_FRAUD_PROOF, fraudProofId,
    64	///         guiltyGuardians)). It binds the proof to (versioned domain name, chainid,
    65	///         this aggregator, its Registry), so a fraud proof accepted on one
    66	///         aggregator/chain is not byte-valid on another. Verifiers MUST bind
    67	///         `domainDigest` into whatever they check; ignoring it re-opens
    68	///         cross-contract replay.
    69	///
    70	///         CC-48 round-5 (MEDIUM-1) — EXACT-SET BINDING IS PART OF THE CONTRACT.
    71	///         A verifier MUST return true only for the guardian set the evidence commits
    72	///         to, EXACTLY. A strict SUBSET, a SUPERSET, and any unrelated set must all be
    73	///         rejected for the same `(fraudProofId, fraudProof)`. "Every listed address is
    74	///         provably a co-signer" is NOT sufficient — that predicate is self-consistent
    75	///         on any subset, and evidence-checking verifiers (as opposed to the
    76	///         attester-commitment reference implementation) satisfy it by construction.
    77	///
    78	///         Why this is a security property and not a nicety: `fraudProofId` is
    79	///         SINGLE-USE FOR EVER. `guardianSlashCases[id].status != 0` blocks re-opening,
    80	///         and status 2 (executed) and 3 (expired) block it just as permanently as 1
    81	///         (pending). `queueGuardianSlash` is permissionless and the guardian set is
    82	///         chosen by the CALLER. So if a verifier accepts subsets, a colluder who sees
    83	///         an honest watcher's `queueGuardianSlash(id, {A,B,C}, proof)` in the mempool
    84	///         can front-run it with `queueGuardianSlash(id, {A}, proof)`: the case opens on
    85	///         {A} alone, executes, burns `id` permanently, and B and C become immune to
    86	///         that evidence while the chain records the matter as settled.
    87	///
    88	///         The gate for this property is
    89	///         `contracts/test/helpers/FraudProofVerifierConformance.sol` —
    90	///         `assertDomainBound` (domain) + `assertSetBound` (set completeness). Both must
    91	///         pass in the DVT repo's own CI before `fraudProofVerifier` leaves address(0).
    92	interface IFraudProofVerifier {
    93	    function verify(
    94	        bytes32 domainDigest,
    95	        uint256 fraudProofId,
    96	        address[] calldata guiltyGuardians,
    97	        bytes calldata fraudProof
    98	    ) external view returns (bool);
    99	}
   100	
   101	/**
   102	 * @title BLSAggregator
   103	 * @notice BLS signature aggregation and verification for DVT slash consensus (V3)
   104	 * @dev Aggregates signatures and updates global reputation in Registry V3.
   105	 *
   106	 *      P0-1 (B6-C1a): pkAgg is no longer accepted from the caller. Pre-fix
   107	 *      `verify(message, signerMask, pkAgg, sig)` accepted any caller-supplied
   108	 *      pkAgg, and the pairing equation `e(pk_agg, H(m)) == e(g1, sig)` is
   109	 *      mathematically satisfiable for any chosen pair (sig, pkAgg). This
   110	 *      allowed an anonymous attacker to forge BLS proofs against any operator
   111	 *      and trigger slash / blacklist / reputation actions. The fix:
   112	 *      1. Public keys are stored as typed `BLS.G1Point` (uncompressed, 128
   113	 *         bytes) along with a 1-indexed validator slot in `[1..MAX_VALIDATORS]`.
   114	 *      2. The aggregator reconstructs `pkAgg` itself from the on-chain
   115	 *         `blsPublicKeys` selected by `signerMask` using the EIP-2537
   116	 *         `BLS12_G1ADD` precompile.
   117	 *      3. The proof payload no longer contains a public key field; it is
   118	 *         strictly `abi.encode(signerMask, sigG2)` (msgG2 is also derived
   119	 *         on-chain via `BLS.hashToG2(expectedMessageHash)`).
   120	 *
   121	 *      Companion fix: `BLSValidator.sol` and `IBLSValidator.sol` are deleted
   122	 *      because the same forgery surface existed there. All callers (Registry,
   123	 *      ReputationSystem, DVTValidator) are routed through this aggregator.
   124	 */
   125	contract BLSAggregator is Ownable, ReentrancyGuard, IVersioned {
   126	    // ====================================
   127	    // Structs
   128	    // ====================================
   129	
   130	    /// @notice Stored BLS validator key. Format is uncompressed EIP-2537 G1
   131	    ///         (4 × 32 = 128 bytes) so the key can be fed directly to the
   132	    ///         G1ADD precompile during `_reconstructPkAgg` without a costly
   133	    ///         decompression step.
   134	    struct BLSValidatorKey {
   135	        BLS.G1Point publicKey;
   136	        uint8 index; // 1-indexed slot in [1..MAX_VALIDATORS]; 0 = unregistered
   137	        bool isActive;
   138	    }
   139	
   140	    struct AggregatedSignature {
   141	        bytes aggregatedSig; // 256 bytes G2
   142	        address[] signers;
   143	        bytes32 messageHash;
   144	        uint256 timestamp;
   145	        bool verified;
   146	    }
   147	
   148	    struct GuardianSlashCase {
   149	        bytes32 guardiansHash;
   150	        // CC-48 round-4 HIGH: the VERDICT is frozen, not the judge. `queueGuardianSlash`
   151	        // runs the verifier exactly once and stores keccak256(fraudProof) here; every
   152	        // later execute/retry re-presents the same proof bytes and is checked against
   153	        // this hash instead of re-asking the verifier.
   154	        //
   155	        // Round-3 froze `verifier` (the address) and re-verified against it. That is not
   156	        // enough: a UUPS/Transparent/Beacon verifier keeps its address AND its
   157	        // extcodehash across an implementation swap, so governance (or a compromised
   158	        // proxy admin) could still flip an already-queued case to `false` and run it out
   159	        // to expiry — the exact round-3 timing attack, one indirection deeper. No
   160	        // on-chain check on the *address* (code.length, extcodehash, EXTCODECOPY) can
   161	        // prove the semantics behind it are immutable, so the address stopped being the
   162	        // thing we rely on. Freezing the verdict also makes the case immune to the
   163	        // verifier selfdestructing, being upgraded to a reverting implementation, or
   164	        // simply going down between a partial execution and its retry.
   165	        bytes32 fraudProofHash;
   166	        uint64 deadline;
   167	        uint8 status; // 0=none, 1=pending, 2=executed, 3=expired
   168	        // CC-48 HIGH-2: a case is only "executed" once EVERY accused guardian has
   169	        // been individually resolved. Partial progress keeps status == 1 so the
   170	        // case stays retryable instead of collapsing into an all-or-nothing batch.
   171	        uint16 guardianCount;
   172	        uint16 resolvedCount;
   173	        /// @dev The slash fraction FROZEN AT QUEUE TIME, in basis points.
   174	        ///      The verdict was already frozen here (guardiansHash, fraudProofHash,
   175	        ///      deadline, verifier) and the price was not: `executeGuardianSlash` is
   176	        ///      permissionless and `setGuardianSlashBps` is immediate, so the caller's
   177	        ///      choice of moment picked which policy applied. Setting 10000 between
   178	        ///      queue and execute reinstated exactly the take-everything behaviour this
   179	        ///      cap exists to remove, on a case adjudicated under a 30% policy; setting
   180	        ///      100 softened a queued verdict 30x. A verdict's price is part of the
   181	        ///      verdict. Packs into the slot with deadline/status/counts at no cost.
   182	        ///      Found by pr-daemon and Codex.
   183	        uint16 slashBps;
   184	        // AUDIT ONLY as of round-4: the verifier that authorized this case, recorded so
   185	        // an observer can attribute the verdict. Execution no longer calls it, and no
   186	        // code path reads it for a decision — a rotation, upgrade, or selfdestruct after
   187	        // queueing changes nothing about the case's fate.
   188	        address verifier;
   189	    }
   190	
   191	    struct GuardianExitRequest {
   192	        uint64 readyAt;
   193	        uint64 expiresAt;
   194	    }
   195	
   196	    // ====================================
   197	    // Storage
   198	    // ====================================
   199	
   200	    IRegistry public immutable REGISTRY;
   201	    address public SUPERPAYMASTER;
   202	    address public DVT_VALIDATOR;
   203	
   204	    /// @notice Validator → registered key. `isActive` doubles as registration flag.
   205	    mapping(address => BLSValidatorKey) internal _blsKeys;
   206	
   207	    /// @notice 1-indexed slot → validator address. signerMask bit `i` (0-indexed)
   208	    ///         corresponds to validator at slot `i+1`.
   209	    mapping(uint8 => address) public validatorAtSlot;
   210	
   211	    mapping(uint256 => AggregatedSignature) public aggregatedSignatures;
   212	    mapping(uint256 => bool) public executedProposals;
   213	    /// @notice Replay guard for queueSlashWithConsensus. A queue proof commits to
   214	    ///         (operator, slashLevel, epoch, chainid); once consumed it cannot be
   215	    ///         replayed — otherwise the same signed proof could re-flag an operator
   216	    ///         after the owner cancelled the slash or after it already executed
   217	    ///         (a reusable withdraw-block DoS). A fresh, legitimate re-queue simply
   218	    ///         uses a new epoch (→ new hash).
   219	    mapping(bytes32 => bool) public usedSlashQueueHashes;
   220	    mapping(uint256 => uint256) public proposalNonces;
   221	
   222	    uint256 public minThreshold = 3; // Floor for the GENERIC executeProposal path only
   223	    uint256 public defaultThreshold = 7; // Default for the reputation consensus path
   224	    uint256 public constant MAX_VALIDATORS = 64;
   225	
   226	    /// @notice Absolute signature floor enforced inside `_checkSignatures` for
   227	    ///         EVERY verification path (a hard safety net; no path may verify
   228	    ///         below this). The generic executeProposal path layers the stricter
   229	    ///         `minThreshold` on top, so lowering the slash floor to 2 (for a
   230	    ///         2-of-3 WARNING) does NOT widen the generic path — that stays at
   231	    ///         minThreshold. Also the min a slash-table entry may be set to.
   232	    uint8 public constant SLASH_THRESHOLD_FLOOR = 2;
   233	
   234	    // ====================================
   235	    // CC-48 round-2: versioned domain separation
   236	    // ====================================
   237	    //
   238	    // Every signed pre-image on every path is
   239	    //     keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), <PATH_TAG>, ...fields))
   240	    // where
   241	    //     domainSeparator() = keccak256(abi.encode(DOMAIN_NAME, block.chainid,
   242	    //                                              address(this), address(REGISTRY)))
   243	    //
   244	    // Before this change a pre-image committed to chainid only. Two aggregators
   245	    // deployed on the SAME chain over the SAME validator keys/slots therefore
   246	    // accepted byte-identical proofs — an experiment-only deployment's signatures
   247	    // replayed verbatim against a production one. Refusing to *configure* the
   248	    // experimental contract is a policy control, not a cryptographic one; the
   249	    // domain now carries `address(this)` and the bound Registry, so a proof simply
   250	    // does not verify anywhere else. Registry re-derives the identical separator
   251	    // locally (see Registry.blsDomainSeparator) — it never asks the aggregator for
   252	    // it, keeping the second verification independent.
   253	    //
   254	    // Bump the *_v1 suffixes ONLY on a breaking encoding change; DVT/SDK pin them.
   255	
   256	    /// @notice Versioned domain name shared by every BLS pre-image in this system.
   257	    bytes32 public constant DOMAIN_NAME = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLSConsensus.v1");
   258	
   259	    /// @notice Path tags. Distinct per path so a proof for one path is never a
   260	    ///         valid proof for another, even with otherwise-identical fields.

    10	/**
    11	 * @title ReputationSystem
    12	 * @notice Advanced reputation calculation and management for the Mycelium Ecosystem.
    13	 * @dev Decoupled scoring logic from storage (Registry V3 holds the final scores).
    14	 */
    15	contract ReputationSystem is Ownable, IReputationCalculator {
    16	    struct Rule {
    17	        uint256 baseScore;
    18	        uint256 activityBonus;
    19	        uint256 maxBonus;
    20	        string description;
    21	    }
    22	
    23	    IRegistry public immutable REGISTRY;

    25	    // community => ruleId => Rule
    26	    mapping(address => mapping(bytes32 => Rule)) public communityRules;
    27	    // community => list of active rule IDs
    28	    mapping(address => bytes32[]) public communityActiveRules;
    29	
    30	    Rule public defaultRule;
    31	
    32	    // Entropy Factor: community => factor (scaled by 1e18, 1.0 = baseline)
    33	    mapping(address => uint256) public entropyFactors;
    34	
    35	    // Future Extensibility: Explicit storage for community sub-reputation
    36	    // community => user => score
    37	    mapping(address => mapping(address => uint256)) public communityReputations;
    38	
    39	    // NFT Boosts: collection address => bonus points
    40	    mapping(address => uint256) public nftCollectionBoost;
    41	    mapping(address => mapping(address => uint256)) public nftHoldStart; // user => collection => timestamp
    42	    address[] public boostedCollections;
    43	    mapping(address => mapping(bytes32 => bool)) private _ruleListed;
    44	    mapping(address => bool) private _collectionListed;
    45	    mapping(address => mapping(bytes32 => bool)) private _ruleActive;
    46	    uint256 public constant MAX_BOOSTED_COLLECTIONS = 50;
    47	    // One-based position in communityActiveRules; zero means absent. Append-only storage.
    48	    mapping(address => mapping(bytes32 => uint256)) private _activeRuleIndex;
    49	
    50	    error Unauthorized();
    51	    error NotAuthorized();
    52	    error MaxBoostedReached();
    53	    error DoesNotHoldNFT();
    54	    error StillHoldsNFT();
    55	    error InvalidCollection();
    56	    error InvalidAddress();
    57	    error InvalidInput();
    58	
    59	    event RuleUpdated(address indexed community, bytes32 indexed ruleId, uint256 base, uint256 bonus);
    60	    event EntropyFactorUpdated(address indexed community, uint256 factor);
    61	    event CommunityReputationUpdated(address indexed community, address indexed user, uint256 score);
    62	    event ReputationComputed(address indexed user, uint256 score);
    63	    event NFTBoostAdded(address indexed collection, uint256 boost);
    64	    event NFTHoldStartReset(address indexed user, address indexed collection);
    65	
    66	    constructor(address _registry) Ownable(msg.sender) {
    67	        REGISTRY = IRegistry(_registry);
    68	        defaultRule = Rule(10, 1, 100, "Default");
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    function version() external pure override returns (string memory) {
    72	        return "Reputation-0.6.0";
    73	    }
    74	
    75	    /**
    76	     * @notice Governance sets the Entropy Factor for a community.
    77	     * @dev 1e18 = 1.0. Lower factor increases "resistance" to reputation gain.
    78	     */
    79	    function setEntropyFactor(address community, uint256 factor) external onlyOwner {
    80	        entropyFactors[community] = factor;
    81	        emit EntropyFactorUpdated(community, factor);
    82	    }
    83	
    84	    /**
    85	     * @notice Set specific community reputation score (called by DVT/Trusted Source)
    86	     * @dev Allows off-chain calculation results to be stored on-chain for specific communities.
    87	     *      Authorized callers: contract owner OR addresses whitelisted via Registry.isReputationSource().
    88	     */
    89	    function setCommunityReputation(address community, address user, uint256 score) external {
    90	        // Reuse Registry's reputation source whitelist for access control
    91	        if (msg.sender != owner() && !REGISTRY.isReputationSource(msg.sender)) revert Unauthorized();
    92	        communityReputations[community][user] = score;
    93	        emit CommunityReputationUpdated(community, user, score);
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    /**
    97	     * @notice Community admins can set their own scoring rules.
    98	     * @dev Restricted to the owner of the community role in the Registry.
    99	     */
   100	    function setRule(bytes32 ruleId, uint256 base, uint256 bonus, uint256 max, string calldata desc) external {
   101	        address community = msg.sender;
   102	        // Verify msg.sender has the COMMUNITY role (multi-tenant: each community manages its own rules)
   103	        if (!REGISTRY.hasRole(keccak256("COMMUNITY"), msg.sender) && owner() != msg.sender) revert NotAuthorized();
   104	        // Bounds constrain rule parameters, not arbitrary activity inputs or entropy factors;
   105	        // multiplication of those inputs may still overflow and revert in computeScore.
   106	        // base/bonus capped at 10_000 points per rule; max capped at 100_000 (max cumulative bonus).
   107	        if (base > 10_000 || bonus > 10_000 || max > 100_000) revert InvalidInput();
   108	
   109	        _ruleListed[community][ruleId] = true;
   110	        bytes32[] storage activeRules = communityActiveRules[community];
   111	        uint256 index = _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId];
   112	        if (base > 0 && index == 0) {
   113	            activeRules.push(ruleId);
   114	            _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId] = activeRules.length;
   115	        } else if (base == 0 && index != 0) {
   116	            uint256 last = activeRules.length;
   117	            if (index != last) {
   118	                bytes32 moved = activeRules[last - 1];
   119	                activeRules[index - 1] = moved;
   120	                _activeRuleIndex[community][moved] = index;
   121	            }
   122	            activeRules.pop();
   123	            delete _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId];
   124	        }
   125	        _ruleActive[community][ruleId] = base > 0;
   126	        communityRules[community][ruleId] = Rule(base, bonus, max, desc);
   127	        emit RuleUpdated(community, ruleId, base, bonus);
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    function setNFTBoost(address collection, uint256 boost) external onlyOwner {
   131	        if (collection == address(0)) revert InvalidAddress();
   132	        if (!_collectionListed[collection]) {
   133	            if (boostedCollections.length >= MAX_BOOSTED_COLLECTIONS) revert MaxBoostedReached();
   134	            _collectionListed[collection] = true;
   135	            boostedCollections.push(collection);
   136	        }
   137	        nftCollectionBoost[collection] = boost;
   138	        emit NFTBoostAdded(collection, boost);
   139	    }
   140	
   141	    /**
   142	     * @notice Compute reputation for a user based on their community activities.
   143	     * @dev Activities are now mapped to rules.
   144	     */
   145	    function computeScore(
   146	        address user,
   147	        address[] memory communities,
   148	        bytes32[][] memory ruleIds,
   149	        uint256[][] memory activities
   150	    ) public view returns (uint256 totalScore) {
   151	        if (ruleIds.length != communities.length || activities.length != communities.length) revert InvalidInput();
   152	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < communities.length; i++) {
   153	            if (ruleIds[i].length != activities[i].length) revert InvalidInput();
   154	            address community = communities[i];
   155	            uint256 communityScore = 0;
   156	
   157	            for (uint256 j = 0; j < ruleIds[i].length; j++) {
   158	                bytes32 rId = ruleIds[i][j];
   159	                Rule memory r = communityRules[community][rId];
   160	
   161	                // A configured zero-base rule is disabled, not missing.
   162	                if (_ruleListed[community][rId] && !_ruleActive[community][rId]) continue;
   163	
   164	                // If rule was never configured, use the default for the first rule.
   165	                // P1-26 resolved: fallback uses constructor-initialized defaultRule; no setter changes it
   166	                if (!_ruleListed[community][rId] && j == 0) {
   167	                    r = defaultRule;
   168	                }
   169	
   170	                uint256 bonus = activities[i][j] * r.activityBonus;
   171	                if (bonus > r.maxBonus) bonus = r.maxBonus;
   172	                communityScore += r.baseScore + bonus;
   173	            }
   174	
   175	            // Apply Entropy Factor (1e18 = 1.0)
   176	            uint256 factor = entropyFactors[community];
   177	            if (factor == 0) factor = 1e18; // Default to 1.0
   178	
   179	            totalScore += (communityScore * factor) / 1e18;
   180	        }
   181	
   182	        // Global NFT Boosts
   183	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < boostedCollections.length; i++) {
   184	            address collection = boostedCollections[i];
   185	            if (_nftBoostEligible(user, collection)) totalScore += nftCollectionBoost[collection];
   186	        }
   187	    }
   188	
   189	    /**
   190	     * @notice Sync computed reputation score to the Registry for the given user.
   191	     * @dev Computes score via computeScore then calls Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation.
   192	     *      The proposalId is derived deterministically from (user, epoch) to ensure uniqueness.
   193	     */
   194	    function syncToRegistry(
   195	        address user,
   196	        address[] memory communities,
   197	        bytes32[][] memory ruleIds,
   198	        uint256[][] memory activities,
   199	        uint256 epoch,
   200	        bytes calldata proof
   201	    ) external {
   202	        uint256 score = computeScore(user, communities, ruleIds, activities);
   203	        address[] memory users = new address[](1);
   204	        users[0] = user;
   205	        uint256[] memory scores = new uint256[](1);
   206	        scores[0] = score;
   207	
   208	        // Hash-derived ID for (user, epoch). Separation from arbitrary external IDs
   209	        // and nonzero output are probabilistic, not structurally guaranteed.
   210	        // Registry independently verifies the full-width ID and enforces user epochs.
   211	        uint256 proposalId = uint256(keccak256(abi.encode("RS", user, epoch)));
   212	        REGISTRY.batchUpdateGlobalReputation(proposalId, users, scores, epoch, proof);
   213	        emit ReputationComputed(user, score);
   214	    }
   215	
   216	    /**
   217	     * @notice IReputationCalculator implementation for MySBT v2.1+
   218	     */
   219	    function calculateReputation(
   220	        address user,
   221	        address community,
   222	        uint256 /* sbtTokenId */
   223	    )
   224	        external
   225	        view
   226	        override
   227	        returns (uint256 communityScore, uint256 globalScore)
   228	    {
   229	        // Find community rule
   230	        bytes32[] memory rules = communityActiveRules[community];
   231	        bytes32[][] memory ruleIds = new bytes32[][](1);
   232	        ruleIds[0] = rules;
   233	
   234	        uint256[][] memory activities = new uint256[][](1);
   235	        activities[0] = new uint256[](rules.length); // No activity data: every allocated entry is zero
   236	
   237	        address[] memory communities = new address[](1);
   238	        communities[0] = community;
   239	
   240	        communityScore = computeScore(user, communities, ruleIds, activities);
   241	        globalScore = communityScore; // Simplification for now
   242	    }
   243	
   244	    function getReputationBreakdown(
   245	        address user,
   246	        address community,
   247	        uint256 /* sbtTokenId */
   248	    )
   249	        external
   250	        view
   251	        override
   252	        returns (uint256 baseScore, uint256 nftBonus, uint256 activityBonus, uint256 multiplier)
   253	    {
   254	        // Mirror calculateReputation: all active rules, zero activity inputs,
   255	        // then the community multiplier and global NFT boosts.
   256	        bytes32[] memory rules = communityActiveRules[community];
   257	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < rules.length; i++) {
   258	            baseScore += communityRules[community][rules[i]].baseScore;
   259	        }
   260	
   280	        return keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_FRAUD_PROOF, fraudProofId, guiltyGuardians));
   281	    }
   282	
   283	    /// @notice Canonical proof-of-possession digest for (validator, publicKey).
   284	    /// @dev    CC-48 round-2 HIGH: the pre-image now binds the VALIDATOR ADDRESS
   285	    ///         (and the domain). Previously it covered only the public key, so the
   286	    ///         PoP sitting in one registrant's public calldata could be lifted by
   287	    ///         any other ROLE_DVT address to register the same key at a second slot.
   288	    ///         With the same key in N slots the reconstructed pkAgg is N*pk, and the
   289	    ///         single key holder can produce N*sk*H(m) — one signer masquerading as
   290	    ///         a quorum. Address binding kills the lift; `blsKeyOwner` (below) kills
   291	    ///         the duplicate registration even when the key holder tries it himself.
   292	    function popDigest(address validator, BLS.G1Point calldata publicKey) public view returns (bytes32) {
   293	        return keccak256(
   294	            abi.encode(
   295	                domainSeparator(), TAG_POP, validator, publicKey.x_a, publicKey.x_b, publicKey.y_a, publicKey.y_b
   296	            )
   297	        );
   298	    }
   299	
   300	    /// @notice H-1: privileged selectors the GENERIC executeProposal path may never invoke
   301	    ///         on any target (caller-chosen threshold >= minThreshold, no proposalFloor):
   302	    ///         - SP queueSlash / executeSlashWithBLS and Registry/DVT markProposalExecuted:
   303	    ///           slash and id-marking go through verifyAndExecute / queueSlashWithConsensus.
   304	    ///         - Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation: Registry re-verifies the proof but its
   305	    ///           domain does not change on DVT rotation and it has no floor, so a generic call
   306	    ///           could execute a pre-rotation reputation proof. Reputation goes through the
   307	    ///           verifyAndExecute reputation branch, which enforces proposalFloor.
   308	    ///         - GTokenStaking slash / slashByDVT: an authorized slasher could otherwise burn
   309	    ///           any stake by any amount, bypassing severity bps, cooldown and floor.
   310	    ///         Whole targets are NOT blocked: Registry.updateOperatorBlacklist (own proof,
   311	    ///         defaultThreshold, blacklistNonce) and SP.updatePriceDVT (freshness, ±20% bands)
   312	    ///         are legitimate generic actions. scripts/check_generic_selector_coverage.py (CI)
   313	    ///         classifies every privileged selector reachable from this path.
   314	    bytes4 private constant SEL_QUEUE_SLASH = bytes4(keccak256("queueSlash(address)"));
   315	    bytes4 private constant SEL_EXECUTE_SLASH_BLS = bytes4(keccak256("executeSlashWithBLS(address,uint8,bytes)"));
   316	    bytes4 private constant SEL_MARK_PROPOSAL_EXECUTED = bytes4(keccak256("markProposalExecuted(uint256)"));
   317	    bytes4 private constant SEL_BATCH_REPUTATION =
   318	        bytes4(keccak256("batchUpdateGlobalReputation(uint256,address[],uint256[],uint256,bytes)"));
   319	    bytes4 private constant SEL_STAKING_SLASH = bytes4(keccak256("slash(address,uint256,string)"));
   320	    bytes4 private constant SEL_STAKING_SLASH_BY_DVT = bytes4(keccak256("slashByDVT(address,bytes32,uint256,string)"));
   321	
   322	    /// @notice Per-severity slash consensus threshold, keyed by SlashLevel
   323	    ///         (0=WARNING, 1=MINOR, 2=MAJOR). Bootstrap (N=3): 2/3/3. This
   324	    ///         replaces the flat defaultThreshold for the slash-only path so the
   325	    ///         bar scales with severity and with the validator set over time,
   326	    ///         by governance table update rather than a code change.
   327	    mapping(uint8 => uint8) public slashThresholds;
   328	
   329	    /// @notice Address permitted to update `slashThresholds`. Set this to a
   330	    ///         multisig for plain governance, or to a TimelockController(multisig)
   331	    ///         to get timelocked policy changes WITHOUT building timelock logic
   332	    ///         (and its bytecode) into this contract. Owner rotates it.
   333	    address public slashPolicyAdmin;
   334	
   335	    /// @notice Fraction of a colluding guardian's remaining ROLE_DVT lock burned per
   336	    ///         `executeGuardianSlash`, in basis points.
   337	    /// @dev    WHY THIS EXISTS. Before it, this path passed the guardian's ENTIRE remaining
   338	    ///         lock to `slashByDVT`, so `lock.amount == 0` was hit on every successful
   339	    ///         guardian slash and `_removeUserRole` fired every time: the contract could not
   340	    ///         express a partial slash at all. With registrations sitting exactly at
  1040	            // proposalId in this contract and in Registry. It needs a real quorum, so it
  1041	            // is not an attack surface — but a no-op shape on a consensus path is a
  1042	            // place for future divergence, and CC-48 is about making the protocol rules
  1043	            // explicit rather than incidental.
  1044	            if (operator == address(0) && slashLevel == 0) revert EmptyProposalNotSupported();
  1045	        }
  1046	
  1047	        // 1. Construct expected message + resolve the required threshold.
  1048	        // The signed message MUST commit to chainid to prevent cross-chain replay.
  1049	        bytes32 expectedMessageHash;
  1050	        uint256 requiredThreshold;
  1051	        if (repUsers.length == 0) {
  1052	            // Slash-only consensus path: bar = per-severity governance table
  1053	            // (2/3/3 at bootstrap), and the signed message additionally commits to
  1054	            // `evidenceHash` so every slash is bound on-chain to the off-chain
  1055	            // evidence that justified it. Keeps the reputation branch (below)
  1056	            // byte-identical so Registry's independent re-verification still matches.
  1057	            if (slashLevel > uint8(ISuperPaymasterSlash.SlashLevel.MAJOR)) revert InvalidParameter("slashLevel");
  1058	            requiredThreshold = slashThresholds[slashLevel];
  1059	            expectedMessageHash = keccak256(
  1060	                abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_EXECUTE_SLASH, proposalId, operator, slashLevel, epoch, evidenceHash)
  1061	            );
  1062	        } else {
  1063	            // Reputation-only path: six top-level fields +
  1064	            // defaultThreshold, so Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation's
  1065	            // re-verification reconstructs the identical hash.
  1066	            requiredThreshold = defaultThreshold;
  1067	            // Byte-identical to Registry._reputationMessageHash. operator/slashLevel
  1068	            // are NOT fields here: they are pinned to zero by the combined-shape guard
  1069	            // above, so including them could only re-introduce the divergence.
  1070	            expectedMessageHash = _reputationMessageHash(proposalId, repUsers, newScores, epoch);
  1071	        }
  1072	
  1073	        // 2. Verify BLS pairing using on-chain reconstructed pkAgg (P0-1).
  1074	        _checkSignatures(proof, expectedMessageHash, requiredThreshold);
  1075	
  1076	        // 2b. A' attribution snapshot (CC-89 stage-2): commit to the signer ADDRESS
  1077	        //     set NOW — after signatures verified, before any revoke can reassign a
  1078	        //     slot — so a later fraud proof can attribute this proposal to the real
  1079	        //     addresses that signed it. Covers BOTH branches (slash-only + rep).
  1080	        uint256 signerMask = abi.decode(proof[:32], (uint256));
  1081	        _commitSigners(proposalId, signerMask, expectedMessageHash);
  1082	
  1083	        // 3. Update Global Reputation in Registry
  1084	        if (repUsers.length > 0) {
  1085	            REGISTRY.batchUpdateGlobalReputation(proposalId, repUsers, newScores, epoch, proof);
  1086	            emit ReputationEpochTriggered(epoch, repUsers.length);
  1087	        } else {
  1088	            // Slash-only proposal: mark proposalId in Registry to prevent cross-path replay
  1089	            // (attacker holding valid proof cannot reuse proposalId via direct Registry call)
  1090	            REGISTRY.markProposalExecuted(proposalId);
  1091	            emit SlashConsensusReached(proposalId, slashLevel, requiredThreshold, evidenceHash);
  1092	        }
  1093	
  1094	        // 4. Execute Slash if operator is provided
  1095	        if (operator != address(0)) {
  1096	            _executeSlash(proposalId, operator, slashLevel, proof);
  1097	        }
  1098	
  1099	        executedProposals[proposalId] = true;
  1100	        if (DVT_VALIDATOR != address(0)) {
  1101	            IDVTValidator(DVT_VALIDATOR).markProposalExecuted(proposalId);
  1102	        }
  1103	    }
  1104	
  1105	    /**
  1106	     * @notice Execute any proposal via BLS consensus (Generic DVT)
  1107	     * @dev Allows executing arbitrary calls to authorized target contracts after BLS signature verification.
  1108	     *      The target contract is responsible for its own access control (checking msg.sender == BLSAggregator).
  1109	     *      Accepted residual: unused pre-rotation generic proofs remain owner-executable;
  1110	     *      proposalFloor applies only to slash and aggregator-routed reputation executed through the current DVT.
  1111	     * @param proposalId Unique proposal ID
  1112	     * @param target Target contract to call
  1113	     * @param callData Encoded function call (abi.encodeCall)
  1114	     * @param requiredThreshold Required number of signatures (must be >= minThreshold)
  1115	     * @param proof BLS aggregated signature proof: abi.encode(uint256 signerMask, bytes sigG2)
  1116	     */
  1117	    function executeProposal(
  1118	        uint256 proposalId,
  1119	        address target,
  1120	        bytes calldata callData,
  1121	        uint256 requiredThreshold,
  1122	        bytes calldata proof
  1123	    ) external nonReentrant {
  1124	        // 1. Access Control
  1125	        if (msg.sender != DVT_VALIDATOR && msg.sender != owner()) {
  1126	            revert UnauthorizedCaller(msg.sender);
  1127	        }
  1128	        if (target == address(0)) revert InvalidTarget(target);
  1129	        // H-1: block the slash / consensus-marking selectors on the generic path (any
  1130	        // target) so a caller-chosen (minThreshold) quorum can never drive
  1131	        // SuperPaymaster.queueSlash/executeSlashWithBLS or markProposalExecuted. The
  1132	        // fund-moving slash goes through the per-severity threshold in
  1133	        // verifyAndExecute; the reversible pre-flag has its own dedicated quorum-gated
  1134	        // entry (queueSlashWithConsensus). Reputation (floor-checked branch) and the
  1135	        // GTokenStaking slashes are blocked too; see the H-1 constants. Generic execution
  1136	        // uses a typed replay key and never writes a raw id in this contract or in
  1137	        // DVTValidator. Selector-scoped (not target-scoped) so
  1138	        // Registry.updateOperatorBlacklist still works.
  1139	        if (callData.length >= 4) {
  1140	            bytes4 sel = bytes4(callData[0:4]);
  1141	            if (
  1142	                sel == SEL_QUEUE_SLASH || sel == SEL_EXECUTE_SLASH_BLS || sel == SEL_MARK_PROPOSAL_EXECUTED
  1143	                    || sel == SEL_BATCH_REPUTATION || sel == SEL_STAKING_SLASH || sel == SEL_STAKING_SLASH_BY_DVT
  1144	            ) {
  1145	                revert ForbiddenGenericSelector(sel);
  1146	            }
  1147	        }
  1148	        if (proposalId == 0) revert InvalidProposalId();
  1149	        uint256 replayKey = uint256(keccak256(abi.encode(TAG_PROPOSAL, proposalId)));
  1150	        if (executedProposals[replayKey]) revert ProposalAlreadyExecuted(proposalId);
  1151	        if (requiredThreshold < minThreshold) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1152	        if (requiredThreshold > MAX_VALIDATORS) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1153	
  1154	        // 2. Construct Generic Message Hash (includes requiredThreshold + chainid)
  1155	        bytes32 expectedMessageHash = keccak256(
  1156	            abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_PROPOSAL, proposalId, target, keccak256(callData), requiredThreshold)
  1157	        );
  1158	
  1159	        // 3. Verify BLS Signatures with custom threshold
  1160	        _checkSignatures(proof, expectedMessageHash, requiredThreshold);
  1161	        uint256 signerMask = abi.decode(proof[:32], (uint256));
  1162	        _commitSigners(replayKey, signerMask, expectedMessageHash);
  1163	
  1164	        // 4. Execute Call
  1165	        (bool success, bytes memory returnData) = target.call(callData);
  1400	            )
  1401	        );
  1402	    }
  1403	
  1404	    function _commitSigners(uint256 key, uint256 signerMask, bytes32 messageHash) internal {
  1405	        proposalSignersCommitment[key] = _computeSignersCommitment(signerMask, key, messageHash);
  1406	        emit SignersCommitted(key, signerMask);
  1407	    }
  1408	
  1409	    /// @notice Canonical reputation-batch pre-image. MUST stay byte-identical to
  1410	    ///         `Registry._reputationMessageHash` — Registry independently re-verifies
  1411	    ///         the same signature, and any divergence turns every reputation proposal
  1412	    ///         into an unexplained `BLSFailed`.
  1413	    /// @dev    Public so DVT/SDK can reproduce it without re-deriving the layout.
  1414	    function reputationMessageHash(
  1415	        uint256 proposalId,
  1416	        address[] calldata users,
  1417	        uint256[] calldata newScores,
  1418	        uint256 epoch
  1419	    ) external view returns (bytes32) {
  1420	        return _reputationMessageHash(proposalId, users, newScores, epoch);
  1421	    }
  1422	
  1423	    function _reputationMessageHash(
  1424	        uint256 proposalId,
  1425	        address[] calldata users,
  1426	        uint256[] calldata newScores,
  1427	        uint256 epoch
  1428	    ) internal view returns (bytes32) {
  1429	        return keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_REPUTATION, proposalId, users, newScores, epoch));
  1430	    }
  1431	
  1432	    function _checkSignatures(bytes calldata proof, bytes32 expectedMessageHash, uint256 requiredThreshold)
  1433	        internal
  1434	        view
  1435	    {
  1436	        // P0-1: proof = abi.encode(uint256 signerMask, bytes sigG2). pkG1 and
  1437	        // msgG2 are NEVER read from the proof — they're reconstructed/derived
  1438	        // on-chain so a forged proof cannot satisfy the pairing.
  1439	        (uint256 signerMask, bytes memory sigG2Bytes) = abi.decode(proof, (uint256, bytes));
  1440	
  1441	        // Absolute floor for ALL paths. The generic executeProposal path enforces
  1442	        // the stricter `minThreshold` before it reaches here (see executeProposal),
  1443	        // so this hard floor lets the slash path verify a 2-of-3 WARNING without
  1444	        // widening the generic path's 3-of-N minimum.
  1445	        if (requiredThreshold < SLASH_THRESHOLD_FLOOR) revert InvalidThreshold();
    90	        entryPointAddr = vm.envAddress("ENTRY_POINT");
    91	        priceFeedAddr = vm.envAddress("ETH_USD_FEED");
    92	        simpleAccountFactory = vm.envAddress("SIMPLE_ACCOUNT_FACTORY");
    93	
    94	        // Resolve Deployer Address
    95	        // 1. Try DEPLOYER_ADDRESS (if set by wrapper script)
    96	        // 2. Fallback to msg.sender (only for Anvil/Private Key mode)
    97	        if (vm.envOr("DEPLOYER_ADDRESS", address(0)) != address(0)) {
    98	            deployer = vm.envAddress("DEPLOYER_ADDRESS");
    99	        } else {
   100	            deployer = msg.sender;
   101	        }
   102	    }
   103	
   104	    function run() external {
   105	        // Use the configured account from CLI (--account or --private-key)
   106	        vm.startBroadcast();
   107	
   108	        console.log("Deployer:", deployer);
   109	
   110	        console.log("=== Step 1: Deploy Foundation (Scheme B - UUPS Proxy) ===");
   111	
   112	        // Deploy Registry as UUPS proxy first (no deps)
   113	        // T-4: EVERY contract below is deployed from its profile.default artifact by explicit
   114	        // path (DefaultArtifacts._deployDefault) and asserted equal to it right after creation;
   115	        // _assertAllDefaultArtifacts() re-checks the whole set at the end. This file imports
   116	        // Registry.sol, so a plain `new` would ship the runs=200 registry-size build.
   117	        Registry regImpl = Registry(_deployDefault("Registry", ""));
   118	        registryImplAddr = address(regImpl); // capture for config write (UUPS upgrade support)
   119	        bytes memory regInit = abi.encodeCall(Registry.initialize, (deployer, address(0), address(0)));
   120	        registry = Registry(_deployDefault("ERC1967Proxy", abi.encode(address(regImpl), regInit)));
   121	
   122	        // Deploy xPNTsFactory early — GTokenAuthorization needs factory address (immutable)
   123	        xpntsFactory = xPNTsFactory(_deployDefault("xPNTsFactory", abi.encode(address(0), address(registry)))); // SP not deployed yet
   124	
   125	        // Deploy GTokenAuthorization (replaces plain GToken)
   126	        gtoken = GTokenAuthorization(
   127	            _deployDefault("GTokenAuthorization", abi.encode(uint256(21_000_000 * 1e18), address(xpntsFactory)))
   128	        );
   129	
   130	        // Deploy Staking and MySBT with immutable Registry + GToken references
   131	        staking =
   132	            GTokenStaking(_deployDefault("GTokenStaking", abi.encode(address(gtoken), deployer, address(registry))));
   133	        mysbt =
   134	            MySBT(_deployDefault("MySBT", abi.encode(address(gtoken), address(staking), address(registry), deployer)));
   135	
   136	        // Wire staking and MySBT into Registry
   137	        registry.setStaking(address(staking));
   138	        registry.setMySBT(address(mysbt));
   139	        // Wire MySBT into GTokenAuthorization (one-time, locks RC-2 SBT path)
   140	        gtoken.setMySBT(address(mysbt));
   141	        // Sync exit fees for ALL operator roles (minStake > 0).
   142	        // ⚠ When adding new operator roles in Registry.initialize(), add them here too.
   143	        bytes32[] memory exitFeeRoles = new bytes32[](5);
   144	        exitFeeRoles[0] = ROLE_PAYMASTER_AOA;
   145	        exitFeeRoles[1] = ROLE_PAYMASTER_SUPER;
   146	        exitFeeRoles[2] = ROLE_DVT;
   147	        exitFeeRoles[3] = ROLE_ANODE;
   148	        exitFeeRoles[4] = ROLE_KMS;
   149	        registry.syncExitFees(exitFeeRoles);
   150	
   151	        // CRITICAL: Must register COMMUNITY and deploy aPNTs BEFORE SuperPaymaster
   152	        // so aPNTs address can be passed to initialize() — avoids 7-day setAPNTsToken timelock.
   153	        console.log("=== Step 2: Pre-register AAStar COMMUNITY + Deploy aPNTs ===");
   154	        gtoken.mint(deployer, 2000 ether);
   155	        gtoken.approve(address(staking), 2000 ether);
   156	        Registry.CommunityRoleData memory aaStarData =
   157	            Registry.CommunityRoleData({name: "AAStar", ensName: "aastar.eth", stakeAmount: 30 ether});
   158	        registry.registerRole(ROLE_COMMUNITY, deployer, abi.encode(aaStarData));
   159	
   160	        // xPNTsFactory already deployed in Step 1
   161	        address apntsAddr =
   162	            xpntsFactory.deployxPNTsToken("AAStar PNTs", "aPNTs", "AAStar", "aastar.eth", 1e18, address(0));
   163	        apnts = xPNTsToken(apntsAddr);
   164	        console.log("  aPNTs Deployed at:", apntsAddr);
   165	
   166	        console.log("=== Step 3: Deploy SuperPaymaster (UUPS Proxy) ===");
   167	        // AUD-4: explicit profile.default artifact (this file imports Registry.sol, so a plain
   168	        // `new` would ship the runs=200 registry-size build).
   169	        SuperPaymaster spImpl = SuperPaymaster(
   170	            payable(_deployDefault("SuperPaymaster", abi.encode(entryPointAddr, address(registry), priceFeedAddr)))
   410	        require(superPaymaster.xpntsFactory() == v55.factory, "wire: sp.setXPNTsFactory(factoryV2)");
   411	        require(xpntsFactory.SUPERPAYMASTER() == address(superPaymaster), "wire: factory.setSuperPaymaster");
   412	
   413	        // ── Step 5: Slasher authorization ───────────────────────────────────────
   414	        // 5.5.0 is disclosure-only: the aggregator's only GToken-stake slash (guardian slash,
   415	        // BLSAggregator.executeGuardianSlash) is unreachable while fraudProofVerifier == 0, so
   416	        // the aggregator holds NO slasher role. Granting it is a 5.5.x enablement step that
   417	        // must ship in the same timelock batch as proposeFraudProofVerifier.
   418	        require(!staking.authorizedSlashers(address(aggregator)), "wire: aggregator must not be a staking slasher");
   419	
   420	        // ── Step 5: SuperPaymaster BLS_AGGREGATOR ───────────────────────────────
   421	        require(superPaymaster.BLS_AGGREGATOR() == address(aggregator), "wire: sp.initBLSAggregator");
   422	
   423	        // ── Step 5: Price feed immutable (baked into SP implementation) ─────────
   424	        require(address(superPaymaster.ETH_USD_PRICE_FEED()) == priceFeedAddr, "wire: sp.priceFeed");
   425	
   426	        // ── Step 6: aPNTs ↔ SuperPaymaster ─────────────────────────────────────
   427	        require(apnts.SUPERPAYMASTER_ADDRESS() == address(superPaymaster), "wire: apnts.setSuperPaymaster");
   428	    }
   429	
   430	    /// @dev Reputation sources are exactly ReputationSystem and the aggregator. The aggregator's
   431	    ///      verifyAndExecute reputation branch (floor-checked) needs the role; the generic path can no
   432	    ///      longer reach batchUpdateGlobalReputation (H-1 denylist). Registry.initialize makes its
   433	    ///      owner (the deployer) a source (Registry.sol:172), so that grant is revoked here.
   434	    function _wireReputationSources() internal {
   435	        registry.setReputationSource(address(repSystem), true);
   436	        registry.setReputationSource(address(aggregator), true);
   437	        registry.setReputationSource(deployer, false);
   438	    }
   439	
   440	    /// @dev Same expectation as the Sepolia manifest (step 13): ReputationSystem and the aggregator are
   441	    ///      sources; the deployer and every other address this script deploys or wires are not.
   442	    function _assertReputationSources() internal view {
   443	        require(registry.isReputationSource(address(repSystem)), "wire: registry.setReputationSource(repSystem)");
   444	        require(registry.isReputationSource(address(aggregator)), "wire: registry.setReputationSource(aggregator)");
   445	        address[22] memory notSources = [
   446	            deployer, address(superPaymaster), spImplAddr, address(dvt), address(staking), address(mysbt),
   447	            address(gtoken), address(xpntsFactory), address(registry), registryImplAddr, address(apnts),
   448	            address(pmFactory), address(pmV4Impl), address(microPaymentCh), x402FacilitatorAddr,
   449	            policyRegistryAddr, v55.factory, v55.impl, v55.ext, v55.lens, v55.tierSource, v55.aoaRegistry
   450	        ];
   451	        for (uint256 i; i < notSources.length; ++i) {
   452	            if (notSources[i] == address(0)) continue;
   453	            require(!registry.isReputationSource(notSources[i]), "wire: unexpected reputation source");
   454	        }
   455	    }
   456	
   457	    function _executeWiring() internal {
   458	        registry.setSuperPaymaster(address(superPaymaster));
   459	        _wireReputationSources();
   460	        registry.setBLSAggregator(address(aggregator));
   461	        dvt.setBLSAggregator(address(aggregator));
   462	        aggregator.setDVTValidator(address(dvt));
   463	        pmFactory.addImplementation("v4.2", address(pmV4Impl));
   464	        // 5.5.0 (runbook step 6): SP binds operators to tokens issued by xPNTsFactoryV2.
   465	        _wireSPFactory(address(superPaymaster), v55.factory);

   182	
   183	`DVTValidator-0.8.0` takes `(registry, firstProposalId)`; there is no predecessor

   186	captures the outgoing DVT's next allocation, including pending IDs, and validates
   187	the successor's aggregator and Registry pointers. Every allocation reads that
   188	floor once. Empty intermediates and A→B→A rollback cannot reuse allocated IDs.
   189	The ordinary setter fails closed if the outgoing counter cannot be read. The
   190	explicit-floor recovery operation requires governance to supply an audited upper
   191	bound on all allocated IDs; it must never lower the stored floor. This recovery
   192	is an immediate owner authority, not automatic discovery of missing history.

   202

   204	`minThreshold`, `defaultThreshold`, and every severity threshold. With one
   205	eligible signer leaving, crossing a threshold is exactly equality with the
   206	pre-exit committee count. Higher unreachable thresholds do not hide lower working
   207	ones; caller-selected generic thresholds above `minThreshold` are not standing
   208	configured quorums. At an exactly reachable N-of-N quorum, seat a replacement or
   209	change policy before voluntary exit or DVT removal. Direct owner revocation and
   210	suspension are explicit emergency overrides.
   211

   214	and rollback and can be set even without local admission or an active key. Use

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Guard the UUPS proxies (SuperPaymaster, Registry) against storage-layout drift.

These two contracts are upgraded in place via ERC1967 proxies, so any change that
reorders, inserts, or resizes a storage variable corrupts live proxy state. v5.3.2
already shipped a broken in-place upgrade because an OperatorConfig field shift went
unnoticed (PR #196). This script snapshots {label, slot, offset, type} for each and
fails CI on any drift, forcing an explicit, reviewed snapshot update.

D5b (spec 03 §10.7b GOV-2 D): byte-equality alone cannot express "only these listed
slots were appended", and `update` overwrote the baseline. The snapshot now stays the
last deployed-compatible baseline and `storage-layout/allowed-changes.json` lists the
ONLY permitted differences per contract:
  - `appended`: exact entries (label/slot/offset/type) inserted immediately before the
    trailing `__gap`, starting at the old gap's first slot, contiguous;
  - the `__gap` must shrink by exactly the number of slots those entries occupy, so the
    END slot (gap start + gap length) is unchanged;
  - `struct_scope_aliases`: a struct whose DECLARING contract was renamed (e.g. moved to a
    shared base) — only the "struct X.Name" qualifier is normalised; every member's
    slot/offset/type is still compared.
Every other difference fails.

Usage:
  python3 scripts/check_storage_layout.py                    # check (CI mode; exit 1 on drift)
  python3 scripts/check_storage_layout.py --self-test        # prove the allow-list logic rejects
                                                             # what it must (synthetic mutations)
  python3 scripts/check_storage_layout.py --negative-control # GOV-2 D: build a scratch tree whose
                                                             # base is OZ Ownable2Step; the gate
                                                             # MUST fail on it (exit 0 = it did)
  python3 scripts/check_storage_layout.py update             # regenerate snapshots (after an
                                                             # intentional, layout-reviewed change;
                                                             # then empty the allow-list)
"""
import copy
import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile

CONTRACTS = ["SuperPaymaster", "Registry"]
SNAP_DIR = "storage-layout"
ALLOW_PATH = os.path.join(SNAP_DIR, "allowed-changes.json")
FORGE = os.environ.get("FORGE") or shutil.which("forge") or os.path.expanduser("~/.foundry/bin/forge")


def _resolve_type(type_id, types, seen):
    """Expand a type reference into a structural description so that nested
    struct/array/mapping INTERNAL layout changes are captured — not just the
    top-level type-id string.

    Critical: storing only the type-id (e.g. `t_struct(OperatorConfig)45043`)
    MISSES a struct field reorder, because the id can stay the same while the
    members shift slot/offset — that is exactly the v5.3.2 / PR #196 bug class.
    We key on the human `label` (no compiler-assigned astId) so genuine layout
    drift trips the guard while incidental id churn does not.
    """
    if type_id in seen:
        return type_id  # cycle guard (self-referential mapping/struct)
    seen = seen | {type_id}
    t = types.get(type_id, {})
    label = t.get("label", type_id)
    members = t.get("members")
    if members:
        return {"struct": label, "members": [
            {"label": m["label"], "slot": m["slot"], "offset": m["offset"],
             "type": _resolve_type(m["type"], types, seen)}
            for m in members
        ]}
    if "value" in t:  # mapping
        key_id = t.get("key", "")
        return {"mapping": label,
                "key": types.get(key_id, {}).get("label", key_id),
                "value": _resolve_type(t["value"], types, seen)}
    if "base" in t:  # array
        return {"array": label, "base": _resolve_type(t["base"], types, seen)}
    return label  # primitive / enum


def current_layout(contract, cwd=None):
    out = subprocess.run(
        [FORGE, "inspect", contract, "storageLayout", "--json"],
        capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=cwd,
    )
    if out.returncode != 0:
        print(f"forge inspect {contract} failed:\n{out.stderr}", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(2)
    data = json.loads(out.stdout)
    types = data.get("types") or {}
    return [
        {"label": e["label"], "slot": e["slot"], "offset": e["offset"],
         "type": _resolve_type(e["type"], types, set())}
        for e in data.get("storage", [])
    ]


def _normalise(obj, aliases):
    """Rewrite 'struct <Alias>.<Name>' -> 'struct <Canonical>.<Name>' everywhere in obj."""
    if not aliases:
        return obj
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        return {k: _normalise(v, aliases) for k, v in obj.items()}
    if isinstance(obj, list):
        return [_normalise(v, aliases) for v in obj]
    if isinstance(obj, str):
        for alias, canon in aliases.items():
            obj = re.sub(r"\bstruct " + re.escape(alias) + r"\.", f"struct {canon}.", obj)
        return obj
    return obj


def _gap_len(entry):
    t = entry["type"]
    label = t["array"] if isinstance(t, dict) and "array" in t else ""
    m = re.fullmatch(r"uint256\[(\d+)\]", label)
    return int(m.group(1)) if m else None


def compare(old, cur, allow):
    """Return a list of problems ([] = cur is old + exactly the allowed changes)."""
    allow = allow or {}
    cur = _normalise(cur, allow.get("struct_scope_aliases", {}))
    appended = allow.get("appended", [])
    if old == cur and not appended:
        return []
    problems = []
    if not old or old[-1]["label"] != "__gap" or _gap_len(old[-1]) is None:
        return ["baseline does not end with a uint256[N] __gap; cannot evaluate an append"]
    old_gap = old[-1]
    old_gap_slot, old_gap_len = int(old_gap["slot"]), _gap_len(old_gap)
    end = old_gap_slot + old_gap_len

    prefix = old[:-1]
    if cur[:len(prefix)] != prefix:
        for i, (o, n) in enumerate(zip(prefix, cur)):
            if o != n:
                problems.append(f"entry {i} changed: {o['label']}@{o['slot']}/{o['offset']} "
                                f"-> {n['label']}@{n['slot']}/{n['offset']}")
                break
        else:
            problems.append("existing entries missing")
        return problems

    tail = cur[len(prefix):]
    if not tail or tail[-1]["label"] != "__gap" or _gap_len(tail[-1]) is None:
        return ["current layout does not end with a uint256[N] __gap"]
    new_gap = tail[-1]
    added = tail[:-1]
    if added != appended:
        problems.append(f"appended entries {[(a['label'], a['slot'], a['offset']) for a in added]} "
                        f"!= allowed {[(a['label'], a['slot'], a['offset']) for a in appended]}")
    used = sorted({int(a["slot"]) for a in added})
    if used and used != list(range(old_gap_slot, old_gap_slot + len(used))):
        problems.append(f"appended slots {used} are not contiguous from the old gap start {old_gap_slot}")
    new_gap_slot, new_gap_len = int(new_gap["slot"]), _gap_len(new_gap)
    if new_gap_slot != old_gap_slot + len(used):
        problems.append(f"__gap starts at {new_gap_slot}, expected {old_gap_slot + len(used)}")
    if new_gap_len != old_gap_len - len(used):
        problems.append(f"__gap length {new_gap_len}, expected {old_gap_len - len(used)}")
    if new_gap_slot + new_gap_len != end:
        problems.append(f"end slot moved: {new_gap_slot + new_gap_len} != {end}")
    return problems


def load_allow():
    if not os.path.exists(ALLOW_PATH):
        return {}
    return json.load(open(ALLOW_PATH))


def check(layouts=None, quiet=False):
    allow_all = load_allow()
    failed = False
    for contract in CONTRACTS:
        cur = layouts[contract] if layouts else current_layout(contract)
        snap_path = os.path.join(SNAP_DIR, f"{contract}.json")
        if not os.path.exists(snap_path):
            print(f"MISSING snapshot {snap_path} — run: python3 scripts/check_storage_layout.py update")
            failed = True
            continue
        # The baseline itself is pinned (Codex D5b review, Medium): `update` rewrites the snapshot, and
        # a rewritten snapshot would make any drift "unchanged". The pin lives in the reviewed
        # allow-list, so replacing a baseline takes a second, explicit edit that shows up in review.
        pin = (allow_all.get(contract) or {}).get("baseline_sha256")
        got = hashlib.sha256(open(snap_path, "rb").read()).hexdigest()
        if pin != got:
            failed = True
            print(f"BASELINE CHANGED  {snap_path}: sha256 {got} != pinned {pin} in {ALLOW_PATH} — a snapshot "
                  f"may only be replaced deliberately (update the pin in the same reviewed change)")
            continue
        old = json.load(open(snap_path))
        problems = compare(old, cur, allow_all.get(contract))
        if not problems:
            n_app = len((allow_all.get(contract) or {}).get("appended", []))
            if not quiet:
                print(f"OK  {contract} storage layout = snapshot"
                      + (f" + {n_app} allowed appended entr{'y' if n_app == 1 else 'ies'}" if n_app else " (unchanged)")
                      + f" ({len(cur)} entries)")
            continue
        failed = True
        print(f"DRIFT  {contract} storage layout differs from snapshot + allowed changes — UUPS-upgrade-UNSAFE:")
        for p in problems:
            print("   ", p)
    return failed


def self_test():
    """Synthetic mutations on the real snapshot + allow-list; each must be rejected."""
    allow_all = load_allow()
    sp_old = json.load(open(os.path.join(SNAP_DIR, "SuperPaymaster.json")))
    sp_allow = allow_all["SuperPaymaster"]
    good = copy.deepcopy(sp_old[:-1]) + copy.deepcopy(sp_allow["appended"])
    gap = copy.deepcopy(sp_old[-1])
    n = len({a["slot"] for a in sp_allow["appended"]})
    gap["slot"] = str(int(gap["slot"]) + n)
    gap["type"] = {"array": f"uint256[{_gap_len(sp_old[-1]) - n}]", "base": "uint256"}
    good.append(gap)
    assert compare(sp_old, good, sp_allow) == [], "positive control: the allowed append must pass"

    cases = {}
    m = copy.deepcopy(good); m.insert(3, {"label": "x", "slot": "3", "offset": 0, "type": "address"})
    cases["insert in the middle"] = m
    m = copy.deepcopy(good); m[1]["slot"] = str(int(m[1]["slot"]) + 1)
    cases["shift an existing slot"] = m
    m = copy.deepcopy(good); m[-1]["type"] = {"array": f"uint256[{_gap_len(good[-1]) + 1}]", "base": "uint256"}
    cases["gap not shrunk (end slot moves)"] = m
    m = copy.deepcopy(good); m.insert(-1, {"label": "extra", "slot": m[-1]["slot"], "offset": 0, "type": "address"})

WHAT IS NOT DERIVED FROM CHAIN STATE, stated because the previous wording         (\\\"COMPUTED from on-chain reputation\\\") over-claimed: the MEMBERSHIP LIST is         supplied by governance. This contract cannot enumerate its own users, so         `seedCreditPopulation` counts exactly the addresses it is handed, and         `expectedPopulationTotal` is checked against that same list -- both numbers         come from the same caller in the same transaction, so the check catches a         truncated calldata, not a short list. A governance operator who seeds an         incomplete roster gets a stock below the truth, and the ceiling it is measured         against is correspondingly loose.         Uncounted addresses are not lost -- the first proposal that touches one books         its whole standing above-floor limit against whatever schedule is then in         force -- but an address that is never touched again never enters the stock at         all. So this bound holds relative to the population governance DECLARED, not         relative to the population that exists. Deriving that roster off-chain (the         `GlobalReputationUpdated` log is the source) and committing to it is the         operator's job; nothing in this contract can check it for them.         WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT COVER, stated plainly because the previous         wording claimed otherwise: the level-1 floor (`creditTierConfig[1]`) is         granted to EVERY address by construction -- including addresses that have         never been the subject of a proposal and addresses that do not yet exist.         That population is unbounded, so NO counter in this contract can bound the         floor, and pretending otherwise is what made the old \\\"sum over all users\\\"         docstring false. Measuring exposure above the floor makes this number a         complete stock over all addresses, because every uncounted address         contributes exactly zero to it. Reported by repo:dvt after hitting it while onboarding three ROLE_DVT operators. Two situations need it: an UPGRADED proxy,         whose population slots read empty while promoted users already exist, and a         schedule change (`setCreditTier` / `setLevelThresholds`), which invalidates         the count it was computed from. Both leave         `creditPopulationSeededAt == 0`, which shuts `updateGlobalReputation`, so         neither can quietly issue credit against a wrong stock.         Governance supplies ONLY an address list. Each address's level is read from         `globalReputation` here, so no operator arithmetic — and in particular no         repetition of the old, structurally wrong \"sum the GlobalReputationUpdated         events into an aPNT baseline\" recipe — can misstate a tier.         `expectedPopulationTotal` is a declared headcount checked against what the         contract actually counted, so a truncated batch cannot be finalized.         Addresses that were never promoted may be omitted: they sit at level 1 and         contribute exactly zero to the derived stock (see `totalCreditExposure`).         A promoted address missed here is self-healing — the next proposal touching         it counts it at its then-current level.         Re-running this after finalization is harmless rather than forbidden: an         address already counted in the current epoch is skipped, so the only effect         of a second call is to count addresses that were missed. Refusing it would         buy nothing and would make the migration batch order-dependent on whether         the proxy had ever been initialized by this implementation.","params":{"expectedPopulationTotal":"headcount governance declares; checked on finalize.","finalize":"declare the count complete and re-open the path.","users":"candidate addresses. CC-48 round-9 MEDIUM-HIGH-B3: the exposure BASELINE is gone from this         signature. This call changes the credit limit of EVERY user         sitting at `level`, so it necessarily changes protocol-wide exposure -- and         round-8 let it do that while `totalCreditExposure` sat still, which is how         one owner call moved the drawable total 16.6x without the protocol-wide cap         noticing. It is not allowed to leave the ledger behind any more: the         population is INVALIDATED in the same call (`_invalidateCreditPopulation`),         which discards the now-meaningless stock and shuts the reputation path until         governance re-counts. There is no \"remember to re-seed afterwards\" step to         forget, because forgetting it stops issuance rather than un-bounding it.         Two writes are exempt, and only because neither can move anybody's credit         limit: re-writing the price a level already has, and pricing a level above         the currently reachable top (CC-48 round-11 LOW-L1). See the branches         below for why each is safe; every write that CAN move a limit invalidates.         CC-48 round-9 LOW-B6: `initialize` documents the schedule as monotonic         (\"higher reputation never lowers credit\"). That invariant is now ENFORCED         against the immediate neighbours, which is sufficient by induction because         every level is written through this function."},"setLevelThresholds(uint256[])":{"details":"CC-48 round-9 HIGH-B1. Same treatment, for the same reason:         the population is DISCARDED and the reputation path shuts         (`creditPopulationSeededAt` back to zero) until governance re-counts through         `seedCreditPopulation`, which reads every level out of this contract's own         `globalReputation` storage. Bumping `creditPopulationEpoch` un-counts every         address at once without needing an enumerable user set.         The alternative considered and rejected was accepting an operator-supplied         re-bucketing (a per-level headcount passed as calldata). It would keep the         path open across a threshold move, but the numbers in it are exactly the         kind of unverifiable operator arithmetic round-9 exists to remove.    321	    /// `ERC20InsufficientAllowance(spender, minStake, minStake + ticketPrice)`. Both
   322	    /// figures live in `IRegistry.RoleConfig` fields 1 and 2 and are read together with
   323	    /// `getRoleConfig(roleId)`; on Sepolia ROLE_DVT is 30e18 + 3e18, so the approval must
   324	    /// be at least 33e18. Reported by repo:dvt after hitting it while onboarding three
   325	    /// ROLE_DVT operators. Separately, an address that already holds `roleId` cannot call
   326	    /// this again for any role except ROLE_ENDUSER (see the `RoleAlreadyGranted` guard
   327	    /// below), so there is no top-up path here for an existing holder.
   328	    function registerRole(bytes32 roleId, address user, bytes calldata roleData) public nonReentrant {
   329	        if (user == address(0)) revert InvalidParam();
   330	        if (msg.sender != user) revert Unauthorized();
   331	        if (roleData.length > 2048) revert InvalidParam();
   332	
   333	        RoleConfig memory config = roleConfigs[roleId];
   334	        if (!config.isActive) revert RoleNotConfigured(roleId, config.isActive);
   335	
   336	        bool alreadyHasRole = hasRole[roleId][user];
   337	        if (alreadyHasRole && roleId != ROLE_ENDUSER) revert RoleAlreadyGranted(roleId, user);
   338	
   339	        _requireCommunityForPaymaster(roleId, user);
   340	
   341	        (uint256 stakeAmount, bytes memory sbtData) = _validateAndProcessRole(roleId, user, roleData);
   342	        stakeAmount = _enforceMinStake(stakeAmount, config.minStake);
   343	
   344	        if (!alreadyHasRole) {
   345	            _firstTimeRegister(roleId, user, roleData, stakeAmount, config.ticketPrice, user);
   346	        } else {

   348	                GTOKEN_STAKING.topUpStake(user, roleId, stakeAmount - roleStakes[roleId][user], user);
   349	                roleStakes[roleId][user] = stakeAmount;
   350	            }
   351	            roleMetadata[roleId][user] = roleData;
   352	        }
   353	
   354	        (uint256 sbtTokenId, ) = MYSBT.mintForRole(user, roleId, sbtData);
   355	        roleSBTTokenIds[roleId][user] = sbtTokenId;
   356	        emit RoleRegistered(roleId, user, alreadyHasRole ? 0 : config.ticketPrice, block.timestamp);
   357	    }
   358	
   359	    function exitRole(bytes32 roleId) external nonReentrant {
   360	        if (!hasRole[roleId][msg.sender]) revert RoleNotGranted(roleId, msg.sender);
   361	        if (roleId == ROLE_DVT) {
   362	            if (blsAggregator == address(0)) revert BLSNotConfigured();
   363	            IGuardianExitGate(blsAggregator).consumeGuardianExit(msg.sender);
   364	        }
   365	
   366	        // M-6: gate real fund release on Staking's source-of-truth, NOT the local
   367	        // `roleStakes` cache. The cache is synced best-effort (try/catch in
   368	        // GTokenStaking._syncRegistry); if a sync ever failed it could be stale,
   369	        // and gating on it would either strand a real lock (cache 0, lock > 0) or
   370	        // brick exit via NoLockFound (cache > 0, lock already cleared).
   371	        bool hasStake = GTOKEN_STAKING.getLockedStake(msg.sender, roleId) > 0;
   372	
   373	        hasRole[roleId][msg.sender] = false;
   374	        _removeFromRoleMembers(roleId, msg.sender);
   375	        if (userRoleCount[msg.sender] > 0) {
   376	            userRoleCount[msg.sender]--;
   377	        }
   378	        _removeFromUserRoles(msg.sender, roleId);
   379	
   380	        if (roleId == ROLE_ENDUSER && userRoleCount[msg.sender] == 0) {
   381	            (bool ok,) = address(MYSBT).call(
   382	                abi.encodeCall(IMySBT.deactivateAllMemberships, (msg.sender))
   383	            );
   384	            if (!ok) {
   385	                pendingMembershipCleanup[msg.sender] = true;
   386	                emit MembershipDeactivationFailed(msg.sender);
   387	            }
   388	        }
   389	
   390	        if (roleId != ROLE_PAYMASTER_AOA && roleId != ROLE_PAYMASTER_SUPER) {
   391	            if (roleId == ROLE_COMMUNITY) {
   392	                bytes memory meta = roleMetadata[roleId][msg.sender];
   393	                if (meta.length > 0) {
   394	                    CommunityRoleData memory data = abi.decode(meta, (CommunityRoleData));
   395	                    delete communityByName[data.name];
   396	                    if (bytes(data.ensName).length > 0) delete communityByENS[data.ensName];
   397	                }
   398	            }
   399	            // P1-32: clear slots so user can re-register after exit.
   400	            delete roleMetadata[roleId][msg.sender];
   401	            delete roleSBTTokenIds[roleId][msg.sender];
   402	        }
   403	
   404	        if (userRoleCount[msg.sender] == 0) {
   405	            if (SUPER_PAYMASTER != address(0)) {
   406	                // M-2: non-fatal — a reverting or paused SuperPaymaster.updateSBTStatus must not
   407	                // deadlock exitRole and freeze the user's locked-stake withdrawal. Mirrors the
   408	                // burnSBT low-level pattern below; the SBT desync is re-syncable, fund release wins.
   409	                (bool _sbtOk,) = SUPER_PAYMASTER.call(
   410	                    abi.encodeCall(ISuperPaymaster.updateSBTStatus, (msg.sender, false))
   411	                );
   412	                if (!_sbtOk) emit SBTStatusSyncFailed(msg.sender, roleId);
   413	            }
   414	            // L-04: non-fatal burnSBT — failure emits SBTBurnFailed.
   415	            (bool _burnOk,) = address(MYSBT).call(abi.encodeCall(IMySBT.burnSBT, (msg.sender)));
   416	            if (!_burnOk) {
   417	                pendingSBTBurn[msg.sender] = true;
   418	                emit SBTBurnFailed(msg.sender, roleId);
   419	            }
   420	        }
   421	
   422	        uint256 exitFee;
   423	        if (hasStake) {
   424	            uint256 lockDuration = roleConfigs[roleId].roleLockDuration;
   425	            if (lockDuration > 0) {
   426	                uint256 lockedAt;
   427	                (,,lockedAt,,) = GTOKEN_STAKING.roleLocks(msg.sender, roleId);
   428	                if (block.timestamp < lockedAt + lockDuration) revert LockNotMet();
   429	            }
   430	            uint256 actualLocked = GTOKEN_STAKING.getLockedStake(msg.sender, roleId);
   431	            emit BurnExecuted(msg.sender, roleId, actualLocked, "Exit");
   432	            roleStakes[roleId][msg.sender] = 0;
   433	            uint256 netAmount = GTOKEN_STAKING.unlockAndTransfer(msg.sender, roleId);
   434	            exitFee = actualLocked > netAmount ? actualLocked - netAmount : 0;
   435	        }
   436	
   437	        emit RoleExited(roleId, msg.sender, exitFee, block.timestamp);
   438	    }
   439	
   440	    /// @notice Retry ENDUSER membership cleanup after a non-fatal exit synchronization failure.
   441	    function reconcileMembershipDeactivation(address user) external {
   442	        if (!pendingMembershipCleanup[user]) revert InvalidParam();
   443	        if (userRoleCount[user] != 0) revert InvalidParam();
   444	        pendingMembershipCleanup[user] = false;
   445	        MYSBT.deactivateAllMemberships(user);
   446	        emit MembershipDeactivationReconciled(user);
   447	    }
   448	
   449	    /// @notice Retry a failed final-role SBT burn.
   450	    function reconcileSBTBurn(address user) external {
   451	        if (!pendingSBTBurn[user]) revert InvalidParam();
   452	        if (userRoleCount[user] != 0) revert InvalidParam();
   453	        pendingSBTBurn[user] = false;
   454	        MYSBT.burnSBT(user);
   455	        emit SBTBurnReconciled(user);
   250	        }
   251	
   252	        // Ticket-only roles have no exit fee
   253	        IRegistry.RoleConfig memory communityCfg = registry.getRoleConfig(ROLE_COMMUNITY);
   254	        assertEq(communityCfg.exitFeePercent, 0, "COMMUNITY is ticket-only");
   255	        assertEq(communityCfg.minExitFee, 0, "COMMUNITY has no min exit fee");
   256	
   257	        IRegistry.RoleConfig memory enduserCfg = registry.getRoleConfig(ROLE_ENDUSER);
   258	        assertEq(enduserCfg.exitFeePercent, 0, "ENDUSER is ticket-only");
   259	        assertEq(enduserCfg.minExitFee, 0, "ENDUSER has no min exit fee");
   260	    }

def negative_control():
    """GOV-2 D: a scratch tree whose shared base is OZ Ownable2Step must FAIL the gate."""
    root = os.getcwd()
    tmp = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="sp-layout-negctl-")
    try:
        shutil.copy(os.path.join(root, "foundry.toml"), tmp)
        os.makedirs(os.path.join(tmp, "contracts"))
        shutil.copytree(os.path.join(root, "contracts", "src"), os.path.join(tmp, "contracts", "src"))
        os.symlink(os.path.join(root, "contracts", "lib"), os.path.join(tmp, "contracts", "lib"))
        os.symlink(os.path.join(root, "singleton-paymaster"), os.path.join(tmp, "singleton-paymaster"))
        oz = 'import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/access/Ownable2Step.sol";\n'
        for rel, frm in [("contracts/src/paymasters/superpaymaster/v3/BasePaymasterUpgradeable.sol",
                          'import "../../../utils/Ownable2StepNamespaced.sol";\n'),
                         ("contracts/src/core/Registry.sol", 'import "../utils/Ownable2StepNamespaced.sol";\n')]:
            p = os.path.join(tmp, rel)
            s = open(p).read()
            assert frm in s and "Ownable2StepNamespaced," in s, rel
            s = s.replace(frm, oz).replace("Ownable2StepNamespaced,", "Ownable2Step,")
            # OZ Ownable2Step has no namespaced-pending helper; drop the upgrade guard in the scratch
            s = s.replace(" _requireNoPendingOwner(); ", " ")
            open(p, "w").write(s)
        layouts = {c: current_layout(c, cwd=tmp) for c in CONTRACTS}
    finally:
        shutil.rmtree(tmp, ignore_errors=True)
    print("negative control: scratch tree with OZ Ownable2Step as the base —")
    if check(layouts):
        print("negative control ok: the gate rejects an OZ Ownable2Step base")
        return
    print("NEGATIVE CONTROL FAILED: the gate accepted an OZ Ownable2Step base")
    sys.exit(2)


def main():
    mode = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "check"
    os.makedirs(SNAP_DIR, exist_ok=True)
    if mode == "--self-test":
        self_test()
        sys.exit(1 if check() else 0)
    if mode == "--negative-control":
        negative_control()
        return
    if mode == "update":
        for contract in CONTRACTS:
            cur = current_layout(contract)
            snap_path = os.path.join(SNAP_DIR, f"{contract}.json")
            with open(snap_path, "w") as f:
                json.dump(cur, f, indent=2)
                f.write("\n")
            print(f"updated {snap_path} ({len(cur)} slots) — now empty {ALLOW_PATH}'s appended lists and "
                  f"re-pin baseline_sha256 = {hashlib.sha256(open(snap_path, 'rb').read()).hexdigest()}")
        return
    sys.exit(1 if check() else 0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
